He was bracing himself for what he had always known: a sudden, dictatorial loss of agency where his body was handled without warning, dignity, or permission. Because he had never been taught the actual mechanics of his own care, he had absolutely no idea how to guide Aubrie, and without his laptop, he couldn't even attempt to explain his needs.

Aubrie, despite her racing pulse and the undeniable butterflies swirling in her stomach, saw the sheer, paralyzing terror in his eyes. She realized instantly that neither of them had a script for this.

"Okay, Jordan," Aubrie whispered softly. She knelt down so she was perfectly at eye-level, refusing to loom over him. "I've never done this before, and I know you can't speak right now. So we're going to figure this out together. Just nod or shake your head for me, okay?"

He swallowed hard, his breathing shallow, and gave a small, shaky nod.

Instead of just reaching for his waistband, she pointed to it. "Do you want me to unbutton your jeans first?" He nodded. She did it slowly, deliberately, ensuring he was comfortable with every single movement. "Okay. Since your legs can't bear weight, do you want to hold onto the grab bar while I lift and swing you over?" She pantomimed the motions, giving him the power to choose.

Once he was finished and safely back in his chair at the roll-under sink, Aubrie ran the faucet until the water was gently warm, wet a soft hand towel with soap, and reached out to help wash his hands and forearms.

The instant the cloth neared his skin, Jordan flinched violently, his shoulders hunching up toward his ears and his eyes squeezing shut as he braced for the harsh, painful scouring and the inevitable shout he had known at every bath for eighteen years.

Aubrie froze immediately, pulling her hand back an inch. Her hazel eyes dropped to his forearms and the edge of his collar, where faint, blotchy patches of raw redness from his mother's rough scrubbing at the motel two nights ago were still visible against his pale skin. Her chest tightened with a sudden, fierce ache as she realized *why* he had flinched. He didn't even know what a gentle touch felt like.

"Hey... Jordan, open your eyes," Aubrie whispered softly, holding the warm cloth still in the air where he could see it. "I'm not going to hurt you. Ever. Feel the water first, okay?"

She waited until he slowly opened his eyes, then let his fingertips brush the soft terrycloth so he could feel that it wasn't scalding. Only when he gave a tiny nod did she gently press the warm cloth to his wrists and forearms—using slow, feather-light strokes without a single ounce of force or impatience, before patting his skin dry with a clean towel.

It was a painstakingly careful process, but she assisted him with a tender, clinical respect that completely bypassed the awkwardness of the situation. For Jordan, sitting there with his heart pounding in his throat, it was an earth-shattering revelation. He hadn't known kindness like this existed until he met Aubrie Kelly. She was asking for his consent. She was treating his body with a gentleness and dignity his mother had never once shown him, and Aubrie found that she cared far more about protecting his dignity than she did about her own embarrassment.

When the door clicked open a few minutes later and Aubrie wheeled Jordan back into the main room, she was met with a wall of knowing, playful looks.

Madison was leaning against the top bunk, a wide, unmistakable smirk on her face. "Wow, Aubrie. You were certainly eager to volunteer for bathroom duty".

"It's called being a helpful suitemate, Madison," Aubrie said defensively, her face immediately turning bright red all over again. She busied herself with straightening the blanket on Jordan's bed, acting as if it were the most engrossing task in the world.

Zoë adjusted her glasses, her tone perfectly deadpan. "The swiftness of your response time was statistically notable," she observed, her crisp London accent underscoring the dry remark.

Hannah just smiled warmly, watching Aubrie fret over the blanket. The entire room had put two and two together. Aubrie Kelly was falling head over heels in love with the quiet boy in the wheelchair, and even if she wasn't quite ready to admit it out loud yet, her blush had already given her away.

### Scene 13: The Spare Hardware

**Date:** 2014-08-23 at 17:45 America/New_York

As the laughter from Aubrie's embarrassed flush began to settle, Zoë turned her analytical gaze toward Jordan's dead, bulky machine.

"The data indicates your current hardware is entirely insufficient for continuous use," Zoë observed, her crisp British consonants cutting through the room's chatter. "A dead battery poses a critical communication failure. Fortunately, I have a logical solution. My family resides just down the road in Christiansburg. I have a spare, fully functional laptop at home."

She pulled out her mPhone. "I will ring my father and have him bring it round."

Jordan's body instantly locked up. His eyes went wide, and his chest tightened with a sudden, suffocating panic. A gift. She was offering him a gift. In Aubrie Sinclair’s household, there was no such thing as a free offering. Everything had a string attached; everything was a transaction, usually paid for with guilt, shame, or severe punishment. He frantically shook his head, waving his hands to stop her, but lacking his text-to-speech software, he was trapped in his own silence.

"It's no trouble at all," Zoë added, misinterpreting his panic as mere politeness as she held the phone to her ear. "Dad? Yes, it's Zoë. Could you do me a massive favour..."

For the next twenty minutes, Jordan sat in a state of mounting dread. It wasn't just the fear of the gift that paralyzed him; it was the realization of *who* was bringing it. A father. He was an eighteen-year-old male sitting in a college dorm suite assigned to five young women, and a protective "girl dad" was on his way. He knew the stereotypes. He expected rage. He expected to be yelled at, accused, and immediately thrown out of the room.

At 6:05 PM, Zoë's phone buzzed. "Right, he's in the lobby," she announced, stepping to the wall to press the square **PUSH TO OPEN** handicap button so the door unlocked and swung open. "I'll just pop down in the lift and escort him up."

Ten seconds after she stepped into the corridor, the motorized arm pulled the heavy wooden door shut until the lock clicked into place, and Jordan's breathing grew shallow. Aubrie, noticing his sheer terror, gently rested her hand on his arm. "It's okay, Jordan," she whispered softly.

A few minutes later, the exterior reader chirped as Zoë tapped her Poly Pass to unlock the latch and pushed the door open. Zoë walked in, followed by a tall, broad-shouldered man with kind eyes and a sturdy, protective presence. This was Daniel Brooks.

"Dad, as I was explaining downstairs, there has been a massive administrative error," Zoë began, gesturing around the cramped space. "Housing mistakenly assigned a male to our suite due to a naming confusion."

Daniel stepped into the room. He carried a sleek, silver laptop case under one arm. He stopped, his eyes sweeping over the crowded space. He saw Mark sitting respectfully on the floor near Hannah. He saw his daughter’s new roommates. And then, his gaze landed on Jordan. He saw the boy in the wheelchair, practically vibrating with fear, his knuckles white as he gripped his armrests, bracing for the inevitable explosion of paternal anger.

Daniel's brow furrowed slightly in confusion. He slowly turned his head to look back at the heavy wooden door they had just walked through. His eyes traced the brass fixtures embedded in the solid wood. He saw the standard peephole at standing height. Then, his gaze dropped lower, resting on the second peephole drilled perfectly at wheelchair level.

The pieces clicked together instantly in Daniel's mind. The university hadn't just put a boy in a girls' suite; they had put a boy who desperately needed an accessible room into the *only* accessible room available on the floor.

Daniel looked back at the five young women, taking in their relaxed posture and the protective, caring way Aubrie was sitting next to Jordan's chair. They weren't uncomfortable. They weren't threatened. They had already accepted this frightened young man as their own.

The tension in Daniel's broad shoulders melted away, replaced by a profound, warming wave of empathy. His heart ached for the sheer terror radiating from the boy.

Daniel bypassed the girls and walked directly over to Jordan. He didn't loom over him; he knelt down so they were eye-to-eye, offering a warm, incredibly gentle smile.

"You must be Jordan," Daniel said, his voice a deep, reassuring rumble that carried the same distinct London accent as his daughter, but with absolutely zero anger. "Zoë tells me your computer gave up the ghost. I'm Daniel."

He gently placed the laptop case on Jordan's desk, unzipped it, and popped the matte-black lid open. Even at a glance, the machine was built like an absolute tank—an enterprise-grade business notebook with a rigid magnesium-alloy frame and a crisp, tactile keyboard. It booted almost instantaneously.

"It's a Pinnacle Vanguard Pro," Zoë explained, stepping up beside her father. "Originally engineered by Atlas before Pinnacle bought their hardware division—built solid to last for years. Dad and I just snapped a brand-new, fresh extended-capacity battery pack into the back before he drove over, so it will easily hold a charge all day. And I wiped Quantum OS off it ages ago; it currently runs a custom distribution of Aura Glade, which is visually identical to what you are used to, but requires a fraction of the processing power."

She tapped the trackpad, bringing up a clean, minimalist text box. "I also pre-installed a program called *EchoType*. It synthesizes speech locally, meaning you won't experience latency issues. And don't worry about network connectivity; the wireless card driver on this model was notoriously stubborn, so I manually compiled a patch in the terminal to force the hardware handshake a while back. The connection is entirely stable."

"It's yours to use for as long as you need it, son," Daniel finished for her, setting the power adapter and the sealed box from the fresh battery gently on the desk. "No strings attached."

Jordan stared at the man, his mind struggling to process the interaction. There was no shouting. There was no accusation. There was just a father looking at him not as an intruder, but with the same unconditional kindness his daughter had shown. In that simple, quiet moment in Room 214, a tiny seed was planted in Jordan's chest—the fragile, miraculous hope that maybe, just maybe, he was finally safe.

### Scene 14: The New Voice

**Date:** 2014-08-23 at 18:15 America/New_York

Zoë set the sleek Pinnacle Vanguard Pro down on Jordan's desk, right next to his old, battered machine.

"Right, let's get you properly set up," Zoë said, her fingers flying across the keyboard as she opened a dark terminal window. "I am creating a dedicated user account for you. And I'm adding you to the sudoers file, granting you full sudo rights. You have root access. It is completely your machine now."

Jordan watched in awe. In his mother's house, he had never been given administrator privileges to anything, let alone his own life.

Zoë produced a small USB stick from her pocket. "Let's transfer your data over. Documents, photos, music, whatever you need." She popped the lid of the old laptop, waking the sluggish Quantum OS 7 from its slumber. After a moment of navigating the file system, her analytical brow furrowed. She looked at Jordan, puzzled. "Jordan, the directories are completely empty. There is absolutely no data on this drive."

Jordan looked down, a hot flush of shame warming his cheeks. He instinctively gripped the armrests of his wheelchair. His mother had never allowed him to save anything personal. He had no photos, no music, no life of his own to transfer. The empty hard drive was a stark, embarrassing reflection of his isolated existence.

Daniel Brooks noticed the immediate return of Jordan's terror. He saw the boy shrink into himself, clearly bracing for an imposing "girl dad" to reprimand him or view him with suspicion. Understanding exactly what the boy needed, Daniel stepped forward slowly, keeping his movements deliberate and unthreatening.

He gently closed the lid of the old, heavy laptop with a soft click.

"Well," Daniel said, his voice a warm, reassuring rumble that carried no judgment whatsoever, "that just means you get to start with a completely clean slate. Let's put this old relic up here on the dresser, shall we? That way, you'll have plenty of room in your backpack for the new one."

Daniel carefully lifted the heavy machine and placed it out of the way on top of the wooden dresser, treating the boy not as a threat, but with a careful, fatherly respect. It was a physical act of packing away his old life, deliberately demonstrating to Jordan that he did not need to be terrified of him.

With the old machine gone, Jordan reached out and carefully pulled the new Vanguard Pro onto his lap. It was significantly lighter and cooler than his old machine. Since it was going to be his primary communication device, he needed to know how it felt to type on it while sitting in his chair. It rested perfectly across his legs.

He logged into his new account. The Aura Glade interface was clean, lightning-fast, and reassuringly familiar. Zoë leaned over to guide him through his new text-to-speech software.

"This is *EchoType*," Zoë explained. "You have several vocal output profiles to choose from."

Jordan scrolled through the list. On his old laptop, his mother had strictly enforced the use of "Quantum David," a flat, grating, and deeply robotic male voice, insisting that anything else was "inappropriate" for him to use. Now, sitting in a room entirely free from her gaze, he felt a quiet spark of rebellion.

He bypassed the male options entirely. He scrolled down and paused on a female voice profile named "Sydney." It possessed a bright, clear Australian accent. He could not explain exactly why he liked it—he had never been to Australia, nor did he know much about it—but he liked the way it sounded in his head. And for the first time in his life, that simple preference was all that mattered. Choosing a female voice felt like a profound act of defiance.

He selected the profile, typed a quick sentence into the text box while the machine rested securely on his lap, and hit the execute key.

<aac>Testing. This is my new voice,</aac> the crisp, female, Australian-accented voice announced to the crowded room.

Jordan instantly tensed, bracing his shoulders. He waited for someone to tell him it was wrong, or weird, or unacceptable for a guy to use a female voice.

Instead, Aubrie’s face lit up with a brilliant smile. Madison let out a cheerful, "I love it!" Lauren and Hannah beamed at him. Mark offered an approving thumbs-up, and Daniel gave a warm, encouraging nod. Even Zoë gave a small smile of logical approval.

The entire room—Aubrie, Zoë, Lauren, Hannah, Mark, Madison, and Daniel—happily and seamlessly accepted his choice without a single question or judgment. A wave of profound empowerment washed over Jordan as he rested his hands on the keyboard. He finally had a voice, and it was entirely his own.

### Scene 15: The Antidote

**Date:** 2014-08-23 at 18:30 America/New_York

With the new laptop safely resting on Jordan's lap, Daniel checked his watch and gave a warm smile. "Right, I should be heading back," he said.

"I'll escort you down to the lobby, Dad," Zoë offered, stepping toward the heavy wooden door and pressing the interior **PUSH TO OPEN** handicap button to unlock and swing the door open. She looked back at Jordan. "I will return shortly to assist you with the rest of the operating system navigation, as you requested."

Mark also took that as his cue to leave. He gave Hannah a quick, affectionate kiss and offered a friendly wave to the room. "See you all tomorrow. Try to get some rest, J."

As Mark and Daniel stepped out into the hallway with Zoë and the ten-second timer began to ease the door shut and re-lock it, Hannah and Lauren gathered their things. With a few cheerful goodnights, they slipped through the connecting ADA-compliant bathroom to retreat to Room 216 for the evening.

A few minutes later, Zoë tapped her Poly Pass to the reader outside, unlocked the latch, and pushed the door open to return to Room 214. She pulled up the wooden desk chair and sat directly across from Jordan's wheelchair. Aubrie and Madison remained in the room, settling onto the lower bunk and the other desk chair, respectively, offering a quiet, supportive audience.

"Let us review the file hierarchy," Zoë began, her crisp British consonants matching her analytical pace. She reached over to guide the trackpad. "Aura Glade organizes directories slightly differently than Quantum OS. Your root directory is here, and your *OmniDoc Suite* applications are located in this executable path..."

She moved quickly, opening windows and explaining system processes. For Jordan, whose brain was already completely exhausted by the day's massive emotional swings, the sudden influx of technical data was too much. The screen began to blur, and a familiar, suffocating tightness gripped his chest. He froze, his breathing turning shallow as he squeezed his eyes shut, his hands dropping rigidly to his armrests.

Aubrie, who had been watching him closely, recognized the sudden tension instantly. She didn't hesitate. She stepped forward, sliding right between Zoë's chair and his wheelchair, and gently wrapped her arms around his shoulders. She pulled him into a warm, firm, and secure hug.

Jordan instinctively leaned into the embrace, burying his face against her shoulder. He remembered the unconditional hug from this morning, the one that had grounded him during his massive meltdown, and he desperately wanted more of it. As he held onto her, a profound realization washed over him. Her name was Aubrie. His mother’s name was Aubrie. But this Aubrie—Aubrie Kelly—was the absolute antithesis of Aubrie Sinclair. His mother's touch was a cage of control and anxiety; Aubrie Kelly's touch was a shield. She was the perfect, healing antidote to the poison of his past.

He took a long, shaky breath, letting the steady rhythm of her heartbeat calm the static in his mind.

"Apologies," Zoë said softly, recognizing the data overload. "I am delivering the information too rapidly. We will pace it."

As Jordan calmed down, Aubrie slowly pulled back, giving him a warm, reassuring smile. Zoë resumed the lesson, this time moving at a much slower, deliberate speed. She showed him how to open the web browser and navigated directly to the CPI Student Portal.

Jordan squinted at the screen, but the layout of the Aura Glade environment felt remarkably similar to what he was used to. He typed a few keystrokes, navigating the portal with ease. He was catching on quickly, the logical structure of the system appealing to his mind.

After another fifteen minutes of exploration, Zoë stood up and stretched. "The data indicates we have achieved a functional baseline," she announced, stifling a small yawn. "I believe I am ready to retire to my room for the evening. Goodnight, everyone."

With a nod, Zoë disappeared through the connecting bathroom door, leaving Aubrie and Madison alone in Room 214 with Jordan.

It was still early in the evening, but the steady drum of rain against the single window and the sheer exhaustion of the day made the small cinderblock room feel like a quiet, isolated cocoon. There wasn't really anything else to do.

Aubrie and Madison exchanged a quiet, meaningful look over Jordan's head. They both understood the assignment perfectly. They could see the lingering, fragile terror in Jordan's eyes—the deep-seated fear that this entire day, their kindness, and their acceptance was just a cruel, elaborate joke waiting for the punchline.

As they sat with him, keeping the atmosphere light and comfortable, the two women quietly admitted something to themselves. They actually liked having Jordan in their room. It wasn't just because Aubrie was already developing a massive, undeniable crush on him. It was because, stripped of the panic and the fear, he was genuinely easy to get along with. He was a calming, gentle presence, and they were more than happy to share their space with him.

### Scene 16: The Trust Fall

**Date:** 2014-08-23 at 20:00 America/New_York

It was 8:00 PM, and the steady drum of rain against the window made the small cinderblock room feel like a quiet, isolated cocoon. Jordan shifted uncomfortably in his wheelchair, a sudden, urgent pressure making him wince. He hurriedly pulled the Pinnacle Vanguard Pro onto his lap, his fingers finding the keys.

<aac>I need to use the bathroom again,</aac> the crisp, Australian-accented voice of *EchoType* announced to the room.

"I'll help!" Aubrie volunteered instantly, springing up from the lower bunk.

The words tumbled out a fraction of a second too quickly. Madison, sitting at the desk, slowly turned around and raised a highly skeptical, teasing eyebrow.

"Shut up," Aubrie muttered, her cheeks instantly blooming into a vivid, glowing crimson.

Madison just grinned. "Actually," she said, standing up, "do you mind if I join you two? If we're going to be a suite, I should probably learn how to help him safely, just in case you aren't around."

Now both women were blushing, a rosy tint coloring Madison's cheeks as the reality of their shared, cramped living situation settled over them. Aubrie nodded, gripping the handles of Jordan's wheelchair, and steered him into the small, connecting ADA-compliant bathroom with Madison following close behind.

Inside the small room, the fluorescent light hummed. Jordan looked between the two women. Even through the blur of a few feet of distance, he could see the lingering flush on their faces. He found himself wondering, with a tentative, fragile hope, exactly how they felt about him. The memory of Aubrie’s warm, grounding hug from earlier that evening was still a vivid, comforting weight on his shoulders. She was the absolute antidote to his mother, and he desperately wanted more of those hugs.

But right now, the physical necessity of the moment was pressing.

As they prepared to help him with the transfer, a sudden, awkward realization hit both Aubrie and Madison simultaneously: they both desperately needed to use the toilet, too.

The radical "no privacy" rule they had casually agreed upon earlier in the afternoon was suddenly put to the ultimate, immediate test. They looked at Jordan, who was medically vulnerable, trapped in his chair, and prone to severe system overloads. A silent, shared logic passed between the two women: they could not risk looking away or stepping out, fearing he might suffer a silent seizure, a sudden fall, or another panicked meltdown if left unanchored.

The clinical reality of their shared space overrode any lingering social conditioning. With a profound, judgment-free respect, they navigated the cramped quarters. They didn't force him to face the wall, and they didn't avert their eyes. They assisted him with his transfer to the grab bars and, out of pure, pragmatic necessity, shared the space to take care of their own needs. It was an incredibly vulnerable moment, stripping away the last vestiges of pretense, and replacing it with an absolute, unbreakable foundation of trust.

When it was over, they smoothly coordinated his transfer back into the wheelchair. Madison took the handles and wheeled him back out into Room 214. The three of them took turns washing their hands at the roll-under sink, a quiet, domestic rhythm already forming between them.

"Okay," Madison sighed, stretching her arms. "It's early, but today has been a marathon. I'm getting into my pajamas."

She pulled a t-shirt and sleep shorts from her dresser and casually began to change right there in the room, true to their pact. Aubrie followed suit, reaching for her own sleepwear.

Midway through pulling her shirt over her head, Aubrie paused. She noticed Jordan sitting completely still in his wheelchair. He was staring longingly at the closed drawer of his dresser where his pajamas were folded, and then down at the stiff, restrictive denim jeans he had been trapped in all day.

Aubrie’s heart broke as the realization hit her. He wasn't just tired. He couldn't get changed. He literally did not know how to do it himself.

She quickly finished pulling her shirt down and knelt in front of his wheelchair. Madison, noticing the shift in energy, stopped and stepped over as well.

"Jordan?" Aubrie asked softly. "Do you... do you need help getting changed?"

Jordan swallowed hard, his hands trembling as he rested them on his laptop keyboard. He felt a deep, burning wave of mortification, but he nodded. <aac>Yes,</aac> he typed quickly.

"We've got you, J," Madison said, her voice completely devoid of pity, filled only with a practical, sisterly warmth.

Working together, they helped him navigate the difficult process. They assisted him out of his stiff day clothes and carefully guided his arms and legs into the soft, comfortable fabric of his pajamas.

"There," Aubrie said, smoothing the collar of his shirt. "Much better. And don't worry about tomorrow morning. We'll help you into your day clothes, too."

Jordan looked at them, his chest tight. A cold, terrifying thought crept into his mind. He waited for the sneer. He waited for the laughter. He was absolutely terrified that this—this gentle, uncomplaining assistance—was just a cruel, elaborate joke, and that at any second, they would realize what a massive, helpless burden he truly was.

Aubrie and Madison saw the raw, unmistakable terror flash in his green eyes.

"Hey," Madison said fiercely, dropping to one knee so she was at eye level with him. "Look at me. Helping you is *never* going to be a problem. Ever. Do you understand?"

"You are not a burden, Jordan," Aubrie whispered, her voice thick with emotion as she rested her hand over his. "We promise."

The absolute sincerity in their voices slowly chipped away at the ice in his chest. He let out a long, shaky breath, allowing himself to believe them.

"Alright, let's get you into bed," Madison said cheerfully, breaking the heavy tension.

They maneuvered his chair next to Bed A and provided the steady leverage he needed to transfer onto the mattress. Once he was settled under the blankets, the room felt incredibly peaceful.

Aubrie leaned over the bed. She didn't hesitate; she wrapped her arms around him in a warm, lingering hug. "Goodnight, Jordan," she whispered into his shoulder, giving him exactly the comfort he had been craving.

"Goodnight, J," Madison called out softly, giving his arm a gentle squeeze before she climbed the wooden ladder to the top bunk, Bed C.

Aubrie walked over to the wall switch, plunging the cinderblock room into a quiet, safe darkness. She climbed into the bottom bunk, Bed B, pulling her own blankets up to her chin.

For the first time in his entire life, Jordan lay in the dark, surrounded by the sound of steady, peaceful breathing, and realized he wasn't afraid. Good night.

## The First Real Harbor

### Scene 1: The Awakening

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 02:00 America/New_York

Room 214 was vast, cold, and unnervingly quiet. The friendly clutter of his new life was gone. He saw his five suitemates—Aubrie, Madison, Zoë, Lauren, and Hannah—standing in the far corners of the room, their backs to him, their shoulders hunched together as they whispered.

He tried to call out to them, to make any kind of sound, but his throat was locked, his voice yielding nothing but silence. He wheeled himself forward, but the floor felt like thick mud, each push of his wheels an exhausting, agonizing effort. As he got closer, their whispers grew, and he could finally make out their words.

"He can't even get dressed by himself," Lauren said, her voice cold and clinical.

Madison sighed, a sound of pure exhaustion. "Such a burden. We always have to help him."

Zoë's voice was sharp with impatient logic. "I still don't understand how this happened. Why is there a boy in our suite? It's just wrong."

"He's just... not like us," Hannah added, her voice full of a pity that felt worse than anger.

Then came the final, most painful cut, from the one who shared his name. "He's broken," Aubrie Jean stated, as if it were a simple, observable fact.

As his heart seized with the panic of being utterly rejected, the heavy wooden door to the room opened. His mother, Aubrie Sinclair, stood there, her face etched with an expression of sad, pitying love.

"Oh, Aubrey, dear," she said, her voice like poisoned honey. "Look at you. Did you really think they would want you? Did you think this 'college' thing would work?"

She gestured to the figures who now stood silently, judging him. "They are just being polite, sweetheart. You're too much for them. You're too much for anyone but me."

She opened her arms wide, her smile now one of serene, confident victory. "But it's okay. You don't have to try anymore. You can stop pretending. Come home. Come back to Mommy. I'm your safe harbor. I am the only one who will ever truly take care of you, because no one else wants the burden."

The figures of his friends all turned at once, their faces blank and cold. In unison, they spoke. "Go home, Aubrey."

He was trapped, cornered by his own worst fears, with nowhere left to go. Demented calliope music began to hum in the background, a chaotic, suffocating soundtrack to his failure.

But then, a new sound entered the cold, vast room.

"Jordan?"

The voice was soft, coming from nowhere and everywhere at once. It was a sound that was out of place, a gentle note of dissonance in the oppressive silence.

"Jordan? It's okay. Wake up."

The voice was a stone tossed into the still, stagnant water. The image of his mother's victorious smile began to waver, like a reflection disturbed. The cold faces of his friends started to blur at the edges, their solid forms becoming unstable. The demented calliope music that had been humming in the background sputtered and died, leaving only the sound of that warm, steady voice. The feeling of being mired in mud was replaced by the real-world tangle of a soft blanket. The chilling dread was being pushed away by a growing warmth against his back.

His eyes snapped open.

The dream shattered completely, leaving only a phantom residue of terror. He wasn't trapped. He was in his bed. And the first thing he became aware of was the source of that warmth, the solid reality of another person pressed against him.

It was Aubrie Jean.

She had heard his distress in the dark, and her voice had been a lifeline, pulling him out of the suffocating darkness. Having gently woken him, she had already turned him onto his side, his back facing her, and had wrapped an arm around his waist, holding him close. She spooned him, resting her chin on his shoulder, her body a warm, solid shield.

"Shh, it's okay," she murmured into his ear, her voice the same one that had just rescued him. "You're okay. I'm here."

Slowly, the last vestiges of the nightmare's terror receded, replaced by the steady rhythm of his own breathing. The nightmare had shown him a false harbor built on control. But here, in the awkward, cramped, unconditional warmth of her embrace, he had finally found a real one.

### Scene 2: No Privacy

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 08:00 America/New_York

Jordan’s first sensation upon waking on this Sunday morning was warmth. It was a deep, soothing heat that seemed to emanate from behind him, blanketing his entire body. For a moment, he was disoriented. He wasn’t stiff, he wasn’t alone, and the recurring nightmare of falling was absent, replaced by a profound sense of being anchored. He slowly became aware of an arm wrapped firmly but gently around his waist, and the soft, steady rhythm of breathing against his neck. The memories of the night flooded back—the nightmare, the whimpering, and then… Aubrie.

He didn’t dare move. The hug she had given him yesterday during his meltdown… that was the first time anyone had ever hugged him. Ever. His mother’s touch was always devoid of affection. That hug had been a revelation, a powerful surge of security he’d never known. This, waking up wrapped in her arms, was an extension of that miracle.

A few moments later, Aubrie stirred behind him. She blinked, her mind registering the cramped space. A blush crept up her neck, but her immediate, instinctive reaction was concern, amplified by the crush that had been blooming since she first saw him. "Are you okay?" she whispered, her voice still thick with sleep. "You were having a nightmare."

Unable to speak the words of gratitude swelling in his chest—and with his new laptop out of reach on the desk—Jordan managed to turn his head slightly, squinting to bring her face into focus and meet her eyes. He gave a firm, earnest nod, his expression radiating a gratitude so immense it couldn't be contained in words.

A cheerful voice from above broke the quiet intimacy. "Well, look at you two."

They both looked up. Madison was leaning over the edge of her top bunk, a wide, knowing smirk on her face. *Called it,* Madison thought, silently teasing her new roommate. She’d seen the way Aubrie’s eyes lit up when Jordan talked about astrophysics. She recognized the look. And now, finding her cozied up in his bed? It wasn’t just about comfort; it was a full-blown crush in action. Guess the 'no privacy' rule is in full effect already.

Aubrie, catching Madison’s look, felt her blush deepen but held her ground. "He was cold," she said defensively, as if it were the most obvious explanation in the world.

Madison didn't buy it for a second. She rolled her eyes playfully and let out a small giggle. "Oh, I'm sure he was," she teased.

Aubrie’s face bloomed into a deep crimson blush. She was caught, but she wasn't about to admit it. Refusing to make eye contact with Madison, she gave Jordan’s shoulder a final, gentle squeeze that lingered just a second too long before she carefully untangled herself from the narrow bed.

The morning proceeded with the now-established controlled chaos of six people navigating a small space. The shower situation was raised by Zoë, and the logical solution was the accessible shower room down the hall. Madison, who also found herself fancying the quiet, kind boy in the wheelchair, readily volunteered alongside Aubrie.

In the spacious, tiled shower room, the reality of their situation was laid bare. As Aubrie and Madison helped Jordan, their motivations added a palpable layer of tenderness to their actions. For Aubrie, every touch was infused with a burgeoning affection. For Madison, her practical nature was softened by a genuine fondness.

For Jordan, the experience was earth-shattering. As they helped him out of his clothes, he was confronted with the clinical fact of their nudity. It was the first time he had ever seen a naked female, and the sight was so wrapped up in an act of profound kindness that it bypassed awkwardness and went straight to a feeling of overwhelming trust. They were helping him, and in her own way, Madison was also fancying him, but he didn't know that. All he knew was that they were there for him, and their care was a balm on the lifetime of loneliness he had endured.

Back in the suite, clean and dressed, the sense of camaraderie was stronger than ever. Madison knelt to tie his shoes, her movements efficient but gentle. When she finished, she looked up and smiled. "All set."

Jordan looked at her, then at Aubrie, then towards the connecting door of Room 216 where the others were. He thought of the hug, of waking up safe in Aubrie’s arms. He wanted—no, needed—to know that feeling again from the rest of his new family. He took a deep breath, his heart pounding with the audacity of his request, and hauled his new Vanguard Pro laptop onto his lap. He hunched over the keyboard, squinting closely at the screen as he typed into *EchoType*. He hit the execute key.

<aac>Madison?</aac> the crisp, Australian-accented female voice asked. <aac>Can... can I have a hug, too?</aac>

Her cheerful expression softened instantly. "Of course, sweetie." She leaned forward, wrapping her arms around his shoulders and pulling him into a warm, strong embrace. It was different from Aubrie's, more solid and straightforward, but just as wonderful.

Lauren and Hannah emerged from their room, drawn by the synthesized voice and the quiet moment. "What's going on?" Lauren asked with a smile.

"Jordan's decided he's a hugger," Madison said, pulling back but keeping a hand on his shoulder.

Jordan felt his face heat up, but he didn't back down. He looked at the other girls. Lauren, who had a longtime boyfriend, beamed and stepped forward immediately. "I give great hugs!" she declared, leaning down to give him a cheerful, sisterly squeeze. "Almost as good as my boyfriend, Tom."

Hannah, who was also in a relationship, followed suit, her hug quieter but no less warm. "We've got you, Jordan," she murmured.

Finally, Zoë, who had been observing with a small smile, stepped up. "My turn, I suppose," she said, her British accent lending a pragmatic but deeply fond tone to her words. Her arms, when they wrapped around him, were firm and deeply reassuring.

Five hugs. Five different girls. Each one a gift. As Madison took the handles of his chair to head for breakfast, Jordan felt an incredible lightness. He was surrounded by a fortress of friendship. In Room 214, a gentle, unspoken romantic tension was beginning to simmer between three roommates. And from Room 216, a wave of solid, unwavering platonic support. He was learning, for the first time, what it felt like to be wanted.

### Scene 3: Worlds Aligned

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 09:30 America/New_York

The university cafeteria doors opened promptly at 9:30 AM for Sunday breakfast. The space was a low hum of activity as the freshmen filed in. As the six of them found a large table by a window, the easy chatter of the group resumed. Jordan settled his wheelchair into the open space at the table. He was still waiting for the punchline, still half-expecting someone to laugh and tell him to leave, but as he watched them pull out chairs and comfortably make space for him, he was slowly starting to understand that maybe, just maybe, this suite family was not a cruel joke.

But a subtle shift was occurring. While Madison, Zoë, Lauren, and Hannah discussed class schedules and debated the merits of different coffee creamers, a quiet, powerful gravity began to pull Jordan and Aubrie into their own orbit.

It started, as it always did with them, with the stars.

"I was looking over the syllabus for Astronomy 101 again last night," Aubrie began, her voice a little softer now that she was speaking just to him. She naturally leaned in closer, knowing he struggled to hear over the cafeteria din. "Dr. Alistair is starting with the life cycle of stars. I can't wait to get into nebula formation."

Jordan’s small smile faded. He flinched, his gaze dropping to the table. He reached for his Pinnacle Vanguard Pro, popping the lid open on his lap. He hunched low, squinting intensely at the screen as his fingers found the keys for *EchoType*.

<aac>Astrophysics...</aac> the synthesized Australian voice mumbled, the word tasting like failure. <aac>I... I'm really worried about that class. My mom... she just... she signed me up for it. I don't know anything about it. The math...</aac>

He trailed off, the full weight of his mother's trap settling on him. He had been set up to fail.

Aubrie looked at him, her expression shifting from simple excitement to a deep, immediate understanding. She saw his fear.

"Hey," she said, leaning forward, her voice quiet but certain. "It's okay. People make it sound harder than it is. It's not just math, Jordan. It's... it's about the patterns."

She saw his gaze flicker up, and she pressed on, speaking their shared, rare language. "People are confusing, right? The rules are always changing. But the stars... the laws of physics, the way a star collapses into a white dwarf... it’s a process. It has an order. You can understand it."

This was it. This was the core of their connection. Though Jordan had no clinical name for the way his mind was wired, he instantly recognized someone who thought the exact same way he did—someone who found solace in systems, in the deep, comforting logic of things that made sense. She was handing him a lifeline.

"Look," she said, her voice full of a new promise. "It's my favorite subject. I'm not going to let you fail. We're in the same class, and the same lab. We'll sit together. We'll study together. I'll be right there with you. I promise."

Jordan looked up at her, his expression full of a fragile, dawning hope. His mother had pushed him into an insurmountable maze, and this girl had just handed him a map and a flashlight. *She'll be right there with me.* This was the light at the end of the tunnel. He saw in her a passion he didn't have, but a logic he understood. She saw in him a mind that worked just like hers, one that could understand, if just given the chance.

Across the table, Madison watched them, a knowing, affectionate smile playing on her lips. She nudged Zoë gently. "Look at them," she whispered. "They're in their own little nebula over there."

Zoë observed them, her analytical mind cataloging the data. The lowered voices, the intense eye contact, the way he leaned into her space as she spoke.

"A strong neuro-social bond is forming," she stated, her crisp London accent and clinical observation barely masking her own complex feelings. "His anxiety is visibly decreasing. It's a predictable and positive outcome."

Lauren, overhearing, just smiled. "It's sweet."

The bubble was finally, gently pierced when Madison decided to tease. "You two are going to be insufferable in that class, you know that? The professor is going to ask a question and you'll both just start speaking fluent astrophysics to each other."

Aubrie’s head snapped up, a deep blush coloring her cheeks as she realized how engrossed they had been. "Oh! No, we were just… talking about the syllabus."

"Mmhmm, the 'syllabus'," Madison said with a wink.

Aubrie wasn't ready to admit the depth of what she was feeling. It was too new, too potent. "He's just... easy to talk to," she deflected, busying herself with her orange juice.

After breakfast, as they walked across the main quad to locate their classroom buildings for the next day, Aubrie naturally fell into place beside Jordan's wheelchair. The crisp autumn air was a refreshing change from the stuffy cafeteria.

Jordan cupped his hand behind his ear, shielding it from the breeze so he could hear her clearly, while he pulled the laptop securely onto his lap to type.

<aac>You really... like it that much?</aac> the digital voice asked quietly, full of a genuine curiosity. <aac>The stars?</aac>

Aubrie didn't have to think. "I do," she said, looking up at the blue sky. "When you look at the stars, I feel small," she said. "But in a good way. Like all the things that are hard down here... they don't matter as much, because we're all just spinning around on a tiny rock in this huge, incredible place."

He looked at her, his expression full of wonder. She wasn't just offering to help him pass; she was offering him a sanctuary. She was showing him the beauty his mother had tried to use as a weapon.

He hunched close to his screen, squinting as he typed. <aac>I... I'd like to feel that way,</aac> he communicated.

As his fingers left the keyboard and his hand moved to rest on the arm of his chair, it brushed against hers as she walked. The contact was brief, accidental, but it sent a jolt of electricity through them both. They pulled their hands away quickly, a shared, shy smile passing between them.

In that look, a universe of unspoken things was communicated. It was a silent acknowledgment that they were two strangers from different worlds who had, against all odds, found they were made of the same stardust.

### Scene 4: The Campus Topography

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 11:00 America/New_York

After breakfast, the group decided to explore the campus together to locate their classroom buildings before Monday morning. As they left the dining hall and ventured further into the university grounds, the physical reality of the Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute began to assert itself. Blacksburg was situated in the mountains, and the campus pathways were carved into the rolling landscape, making it notoriously hilly.

Aubrie naturally fell into place behind Jordan's wheelchair, her hands gripping the handles. The path toward the main academic quad started with a gentle slope, but it quickly steepened into a long, grueling incline.

Within a couple of hundred yards, Aubrie's breathing grew heavy. Her pace slowed significantly as she threw her weight against the chair, her stubborn loyalty pushing her forward even as her stamina rapidly drained.

Jordan felt the chair's momentum drag. He twisted around, squinting up at her. Though Aubrie's face was blurry, he could hear her labored, panting breath. A familiar, suffocating knot of guilt instantly formed in his stomach. He was a burden. He instinctively grabbed the metal push-rings of his wheels, trying to use his own limited upper-body strength to help propel the chair up the hill, though the uncooperative stiffness in his hands and arms made the effort exhausting and clumsy, his muscles trembling under the strain.

With his laptop zipped away in his backpack, he had no way to speak at all. He couldn't tell her to stop, he couldn't apologize, and he had absolutely no idea that he was allowed to ask anyone else in the group for help. He just pushed harder, his head pounding.

Hannah, walking a few paces behind with Lauren, watched the silent struggle unfold. Her sharp, "mom friend" instincts kicked in immediately. She saw Aubrie's fierce but fading determination and Jordan's frantic, guilty attempts to push his own weight. She realized instantly that neither of them was going to ask for a break.

"Aubrie, hold up a second," Hannah called out, her voice warm but carrying a gentle, unyielding authority. She stepped smoothly behind the wheelchair. "You're going to burn yourself out before we even find the science buildings. Let me take a turn."

Aubrie, chest heaving, reluctantly stepped aside, her face flushed bright red from the exertion.

Hannah gripped the handles. With her sturdy, capable build, she leaned into the incline. The wheelchair surged forward with a smooth, steady momentum that shocked Jordan. He let go of his push-rings, his arms dropping to his lap as he looked up at Hannah in awe.

"We've got to pace ourselves on these hills," Lauren chimed in, moving to walk casually beside Jordan's chair. She wasn't even out of breath. "Hannah and I can handle the steep parts. We'll just rotate. You and Aubrie take the flat stretches."

Jordan looked from Hannah's easy, effortless smile to Lauren's reassuring, gentle gaze. He didn't need his text-to-speech software to communicate his profound relief. The crushing weight of feeling like a physical burden lifted. He was learning the difficult topography of his new home, but more importantly, he was discovering that this new family had the strength to carry him over the steepest mountains without him ever having to ask.

### Scene 5: A Different Kind of Constellation

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 13:00 America/New_York

While Jordan remained in the quiet sanctuary of Room 214, carefully organizing his heavy astrophysics textbooks on his desk, the five women congregated next door in Room 216. The connecting bathroom doors were shut, giving them a rare moment of privacy.

Madison, sitting cross-legged on the lower bunk, was the first to address the massive, undeniable gravitational pull that had formed during breakfast.

"Alright, we need to talk about the elephant in the room," Madison announced, a wide, affectionate grin spreading across her face. "Or rather, the nebula in the cafeteria. Aubrie, you are completely, helplessly smitten with him."

Aubrie, who was leaning against the cinderblock wall, didn't even try to deny it. A deep, honest flush colored her cheeks, but she met Madison's gaze head-on. "I am," she confessed, her voice soft but entirely certain. "I am completely gone for him. He's... he thinks the way I do. When he looks at me, I don't feel like I have to translate myself."

"It is visibly obvious," Zoë added from the desk chair, her crisp British consonants clipping through the air. "Your heart rate elevates when you touch his wheelchair, and your protective instincts are operating at maximum capacity."

Aubrie smiled, but her expression quickly grew serious as she looked around the cramped room. She took a deep breath.

"But I'm not blind," Aubrie said, looking directly at Madison, and then to Zoë. "Madison, you volunteered to help him in the bathroom before I could even get the words out, and you tied his shoes this morning like it was a privilege. And Zoë, you casually handed him a thousand-dollar piece of hardware last night just to make sure he felt safe."

"It was an unused Vanguard Pro running a lightweight, custom distribution of Aura Glade," Zoë corrected smoothly, though a faint pink tinge appeared on her own cheeks. "It was a simple, logical reallocation of resources."

"It was because you fancy him," Aubrie countered gently. "Both of you do."

Silence fell over the small room. Madison looked down at her hands, while Zoë suddenly found a loose thread on her jeans absolutely fascinating. In a traditional college dorm, this was the exact moment where lines were drawn, alliances were formed, and a toxic, dramatic love triangle was born.

Aubrie refused to let that happen.

"Jealousy feels selfish," Aubrie explained passionately, stepping into the center of the room. "Look at what he's been through. He has spent his entire life being isolated, abused, and made to feel like he is nothing but a massive burden. His mother controlled everything he did. How could we possibly turn around and make him the center of a bunch of territorial drama or competition?"

She looked at her suitemates, her eyes shining with a radical, neurodivergent logic. "What if we just... don't compete? What if we don't force him to choose, or make him feel guilty for accepting comfort? What if we all just love him, and support him, equally?"

Madison’s head snapped up, her bright blue eyes widening as the concept clicked into place. The tension in her shoulders vanished. "You mean... you wouldn't be mad? If I wanted to hold his hand, or cuddle with him?"

"I would be thrilled," Aubrie said, her voice fiercely sincere. "Because it means he's getting more love, and more anchors. He needs all the safe harbors he can get."

Zoë adjusted her glasses, her analytical mind rapidly processing the emotional logistics. "A poly-affectionate dynamic," she murmured, her British accent lending a clinical gravity to the profound sentiment. "It eliminates the stress of competition and maximizes his emotional support network. It is... an incredibly stable system."

"I think it's beautiful," Lauren chimed in from the top bunk, her voice warm and gentle. "I mean, obviously, I have Tom. We're really serious, and I love him, so I am strictly in the platonic camp here. But Jordan deserves exactly this kind of safety. I'm fully on board to be the protective sister."

Hannah, sitting beside Zoë, nodded in firm agreement. As the oldest of the group, her "mom friend" instincts were already deeply invested in Jordan's well-being. "Mark and I are solid," she said with a warm, grounded smile. "But Jordan needs a family. I'm with Lauren. I'm his sister now, and I will fiercely protect whatever beautiful, unconventional thing you three are building with him."

A silent, powerful agreement bloomed in the cinderblock room. The traditional rules of dating and jealousy had been completely discarded, replaced by a unique, unbreakable pact of mutual respect and radical generosity. They were not rivals. They were a constellation, and their collective purpose was to illuminate his world and ensure he never had to brave the dark alone again.

### Scene 6: The Safe Harbor's Perimeter

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 16:30 America/New_York

By late afternoon, the golden hour light was beginning to filter through the single window of Room 214. Jordan had just finished neatly aligning his textbooks on his desk when a rhythmic knock sounded on the heavy wooden door.

Madison reached over and pressed the square **PUSH TO OPEN** handicap button on the interior wall, unlocking the latch so the motorized opener swung the door inward. Mark stepped into the suite, bringing with him a wave of easy, confident energy as the ten-second timer counted down and eased the door shut behind him. He immediately spotted Hannah emerging from the connecting bathroom. His face lit up, and he crossed the room, wrapping his arms around her in a warm, familiar hug before giving her a polite, affectionate kiss.

"Hey," Mark said softly, leaning his forehead against hers for a brief second. "Ready for dinner?"

"Almost," Hannah smiled, keeping one hand resting comfortably on his chest. She glanced back toward Room 216, where Lauren, Zoë, and Aubrie were gathering their jackets, then looked over at Jordan, who was sitting quietly in his wheelchair.

Hannah stepped back from Mark, her expression turning serious but warm. "Hey, before we head out... I need to fill you in on a suite consensus we reached this afternoon. About Jordan."

Mark’s posture shifted, his full attention locking onto his girlfriend. "Is everything okay?"

"Everything is perfect," Hannah assured him, keeping her voice low so as not to overwhelm Jordan, but clear enough that Mark could hear every word. She quickly and efficiently explained the "Different Kind of Constellation" the women had agreed upon. She laid out the unique, poly-affectionate dynamic forming between Jordan, Aubrie, Madison, and Zoë, and clarified her and Lauren's roles as the fierce, platonic protectors of the group.

Mark listened intently, his broad shoulders relaxed. He didn't interrupt, and he didn't laugh. As an engineering student, he appreciated logic, but as a genuinely good person, he possessed a deep well of empathy. He looked past Hannah to the young man in the wheelchair—a boy who had been emotionally starved and physically isolated for his entire life.

When Hannah finished, Mark gave a slow, respectful nod.

"I think that's incredible," Mark said, his voice entirely sincere. "He needs a family, Han. And he couldn't ask for a better, safer group of people to provide it. I am one hundred percent on board."

Hannah beamed, her love for Mark deepening in that single moment of pure, unselfish understanding.

Mark then looked down at the heavy, specialized tires on Jordan's wheelchair, his practical mind shifting to the physical realities of moving as a group. "I'm glad you all have this locked in, because getting around outside is a completely different logistical challenge. I've been taking the Radford Connector over here all week," Mark noted, looking around the room. "I caught the Radford Connector from the McConnell Transit Center over to Commonwealth Mall a few times before move-in just to get the lay of the land again. Plus, I spent half my childhood at Forger Summer Camp right here on campus. I know these hills intimately."

Zoë adjusted her glasses, her analytical mind immediately recognizing the value of his data. "Excellent. You can serve as our primary navigator. We require the most efficient, least severely inclined routes between the residential sector and the dining halls."

"You got it. I'll lead the way," Mark smiled. "This place is essentially a mountain range. Who's on hill duty?"

"Lauren and I," Hannah answered proudly. "We figured it out on the way back from breakfast. Aubrie has the heart for it, but she burns out on the inclines. Lauren and I have the stamina."

Mark chuckled, a sound of genuine respect. "Makes perfect sense. You two are definitely the strongest of the pack. If you ever need a sub, you just let me know. I've got your backs."

"Alright, suite family," Madison announced, clapping her hands together as she stepped into the main room. "Let's go get some food."

As the group began to move toward the door, Hannah walked over to Jordan. He had his Pinnacle Vanguard Pro resting on his lap, ready to travel.

"Ready for dinner, J?" Hannah asked cheerfully.

Before he could type a response, Hannah leaned down and wrapped her arms securely around his shoulders, pulling him into a tight, lingering hug.

Instantly, Jordan’s entire body went rigid. His breath hitched in his throat, and his eyes darted frantically over Hannah's shoulder to where Mark was standing by the door.

In Jordan's limited, terrifying worldview, he knew exactly what happened when a man saw someone else touching their property. His mother had taught him that the world was territorial, aggressive, and deeply transactional. He was an eighteen-year-old male receiving physical affection from another man’s girlfriend, right in front of him.

Panic flared in Jordan's chest. He braced himself for the shout. He waited for Mark's face to twist into a mask of territorial rage, for the heavy footsteps marching across the room, for the inevitable physical removal from the suite.

But the shout never came.

Mark was leaning casually against the doorframe. He wasn't puffing out his chest, and his jaw wasn't clenched. Instead, Mark was watching them with a soft, genuine smile. He saw the sheer terror in Jordan's wide green eyes, and he understood it immediately.

Mark didn't rush forward to establish dominance. He took a slow, relaxed step into the room, keeping his hands visibly out of his pockets and his body language entirely unthreatening.

Hannah pulled back from the hug, giving Jordan's shoulder a final, affectionate squeeze.

Mark stepped up beside her. He looked down at Jordan, his friendly demeanor radiating a quiet, unshakeable security.

"Hannah's a world-class hugger, man," Mark said easily, his tone light and completely devoid of jealousy. "You're in good hands with her."

Mark held out his hand, offering a gentle, low-pressure fist bump.

Jordan stared at the extended fist, his mind struggling to process the absolute absence of anger. Slowly, with a trembling hand, Jordan reached out and tapped his knuckles against Mark's.

"Let's go get some burgers. I know a shortcut around the library that bypasses the worst of the steps," Mark smiled, pressing the **PUSH TO OPEN** handicap button on the interior wall so the door unlocked and swung wide open on its ten-second timer for the entire group.

As Aubrie took the handles of the wheelchair and pushed Jordan out into the hallway, the tight, suffocating knot in Jordan's chest finally began to loosen. Mark wasn't angry. He wasn't jealous. He was just... happy that Jordan was being cared for. It was a profound, quiet revelation that etched itself permanently into Jordan's memory, establishing a baseline of what a real, healthy relationship looked like.

### Scene 7: Kincaid Hall

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 17:15 America/New_York

The walk to Kincaid Hall was infinitely easier with Mark leading the way. True to his word, he navigated the group along a winding, paved path that bypassed the steepest ridges of the campus, making the trip smooth and entirely manageable for Hannah and Lauren as they took turns pushing Jordan’s wheelchair.

Kincaid Hall was the massive, sprawling student center at the heart of campus, serving as a hub for dining, student organizations, and a constant, chaotic flow of freshmen. The cafeteria inside was brightly lit and echoing with the sound of a thousand overlapping conversations.

Mark quickly spotted a large, circular table near the floor-to-ceiling windows and led the suite family over to claim it. While the rest of the group settled in and Jordan pulled his Vanguard Pro onto his lap to look over the menus, Lauren realized she hadn't grabbed a drink.

"I'll be right back, guys," Lauren smiled, setting her purse on a chair. "Just going to hit the fountain."

Lauren navigated her way back toward the crowded beverage station, expertly dodging students carrying heavy trays. As she approached the soda dispensers, a familiar profile caught her eye near the deli line.

It was Tom.

Lauren’s heart did a familiar, happy flutter, but she paused. He was standing face-to-face with a young woman with dark hair pulled into a messy ponytail. Over the din of the cafeteria, Lauren caught a brief snippet of the woman’s voice—a bright, unmistakable Australian accent.

Before Lauren could even call his name, Tom shifted his weight and caught sight of Lauren approaching. His relaxed posture snapped into something rigid and panicked. He said a few rapid, hurried words to the girl—so fast and sharp it looked entirely out of character for him—and abruptly turned his back on her, stepping directly into Lauren's path.

The girl with the Australian accent blinked, visibly taken aback by the sudden, dismissive shift in her boyfriend of two years. She stared at the back of his head, her brow furrowing in confusion, before shaking her head and walking away into the crowd.

"Tom!" Lauren beamed, throwing her arms around his neck, entirely focused on the boy she loved.

"Hey, beautiful," Tom replied smoothly, catching her by the waist. His smile was bright and perfect, masking the terrified adrenaline spiking in his chest. "I didn't know you were eating at Kincaid tonight".

"We just got here," Lauren said, leaning back to look up at him. She glanced over her shoulder, trying to spot the dark-haired girl in the crowd, but she was already gone. Lauren looked back at Tom, her curiosity piqued. "Who was that you were talking to? She sounded like she had a really strong accent".

Tom didn't miss a single beat. He let out a soft, dismissive chuckle, his eyes locking onto Lauren's with absolute, unwavering sincerity.

"Her? Nobody, really," Tom said, his tone perfectly casual. He brushed a stray strand of hair behind Lauren's ear. "Just a former classmate from my hometown".

### Scene 8: The Interception

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 17:25 America/New_York

"Come on," Lauren said, grabbing Tom's hand. "You have to come meet my suitemates".

"Hey, Lauren," a voice interrupted.

Mark stepped up to the beverage station, holding two empty cups. "Hannah wanted to know if you could grab some extra napkins on your way back".

Lauren paused, turning to Mark with a bright smile. "Oh, perfect timing! Mark, this is my boyfriend, Tom. Tom, this is Mark, Hannah's boyfriend".

Tom extended his hand, instantly radiating an easy, effortless charm. "Good to meet you, man," Tom said.

But the physical reality of the introduction immediately betrayed him. Mark stood at a solid 5'8" with a broad-shouldered, muscular build. Tom, still stinging from his total failure to physically dominate the towering Australian woman moments earlier, launched into an exercise of desperate overcompensation. His handshake was forced and overly firm, his chest puffed out to a brittle extreme as he tried to claw back the masculinity he felt he had just lost.

"Likewise," Mark smiled, returning the firm handshake, though his sharp eyes easily registered the aggressive posturing. "You an engineering major too?"

"Nah, business," Tom replied. "But I'm dreading the humanities requirement. I ended up stuck in an Intro to Political Science lecture on Monday afternoons".

"Wait, really? Dr. Harrison’s class at 2:00 PM?" Mark asked.

"That's the one," Tom laughed.

"No way, I'm in that same section," Mark said, pulling his mPhone from his pocket. "We should swap numbers and be study partners".

"Absolutely, man," Tom agreed, pulling out his own phone to exchange contact info.

Lauren beamed, watching the two most important guys in her and Hannah's lives hitting it off. "Okay, now that you two are best friends," she teased, "Tom, let's go. They're waiting for us".

Just as Tom put his phone away, it buzzed loudly in his hand. He glanced at the screen and let out a small, apologetic sigh.

"Ah, babe, I'm so sorry. I hate to eat and run, but I've got to bounce," Tom announced, slipping the phone back into his pocket. "My roommate is waiting on me outside".

He offered a general, charming wave to Mark. "It was great meeting you, man. I'll see you on Monday".

Tom turned to Lauren to say goodbye. She stood up on her tiptoes, smiling as she wrapped her arms around his neck. Tom returned the embrace, pulling her in.

But as Lauren pressed her cheek against his chest, something felt off. His arms were around her, but his shoulders were rigid. Instead of melting into the embrace, his posture was strangely defensive, almost performative. She could feel his chest expanding with shallow, tense breaths, and she distinctly felt the movement of his head as he looked over her shoulder, his eyes actively scanning the crowded cafeteria behind her.

He patted her back twice—a rapid, staccato rhythm—and quickly stepped away, breaking the physical contact almost before it had truly begun.

"I'll call you tonight," Tom promised, his smile bright and flawless once again. He gave her a quick kiss on the forehead and quickly navigated his way through the crowds, heading for the exit without ever laying eyes on the table where Jordan was sitting.

Beside her, Mark took a slow sip of his drink, his sharp, observant eyes lingering on the doors where Tom had just disappeared. Mark had seen the hug, too. He had noticed the way Tom’s eyes darted toward the exits, scanning the room like a man making sure the coast was clear. He had seen the rigid, perfectly managed way Tom had stepped away from Lauren. It was the body language of someone carefully managing a situation, not someone saying a relaxed goodbye to the girl they loved.

Mark didn't say a word to Lauren, not wanting to ruin her evening. He just set his cup down, making a quiet, permanent mental note of the tension, and resolved to keep a very close eye on his new Political Science study partner.

### Scene 9: The View from the Fifth Floor

**Date:** 2014-08-24 at 18:00 America/New_York

The elevator ride up to the fifth floor of Bennett Hall was a slow, rattling crawl, but Sarah Braithwaite didn't mind. She leaned against the metal handrail, a lingering smile playing on her lips from her walk back from Kincaid Hall.

When the doors finally chimed open, she stepped out into the humid, chaotic corridor. The odd-numbered suites along their side of the fifth floor were entirely female, and with move-in weekend still wrapping up, the hallway was a maze of broken-down cardboard boxes and propped-open wooden doors.

Sarah tapped her Poly Pass to the reader to unlock Room 505 and pushed the door open. The layout was identical to the suites on the lower floors, but heavily crowded. Like the rest of the standard suites in Bennett Hall, it was a two-person room that had been force-tripled by the university's housing crisis. There was a bunk bed against one cinderblock wall, a single Twin XL bed against the other, and almost zero floor space—shared by Sarah, her fellow Australian roommate Jessica Gallagher, and their third roommate, Chloe from Richmond.

Jessica was sitting cross-legged on the bottom bunk, attempting to organize a massive pile of textbooks on her lap.

"Hey," Jessica sighed, looking up as Sarah squeezed through the doorway. "Did you see the bathroom yet? The girls in 507—Ashley, Emily, and Cassie—just moved all their shower caddies in. Six of us sharing one toilet and two sinks. It is going to be a logistical nightmare."

"We'll just have to draw up a schedule, mate," Sarah laughed, dropping her backpack onto her desk. "Military precision. Five minutes in, five minutes out."

"Easy for you to say," Jessica groaned, though she was smiling. "You're always in a good mood. How was Tom?"

Sarah’s expression softened instantly. "He's good. I ran into him at Kincaid Commons right before he had to rush off to meet his roommate and finish unpacking. He said his roommate is driving him crazy with all the boxes."

"Two years, right?" Jessica asked, leaning her head back against the cinderblock wall. "You guys have been together since high school?"

"Yeah," Sarah nodded, unzipping her bag to put away her notebooks. "Two years. It’s funny... moving to a new country was so chaotic. When my dad and I settled in Radford, everything just felt upside down. But Tom... he was the first person who just made sense here. He’s always been so steady."

She thought back to the brief interaction near the beverage station at Kincaid Commons. Tom had brushed off that clingy girl from his hometown so quickly just to come say hello to her, before ducking out to meet his roommate. It was a small thing, but it was exactly why she trusted him. He didn't entertain drama, and he always made her feel like a priority.

Jessica tossed a notebook onto her desk. "Well, I'm jealous. Trying to date on this campus is going to be a nightmare. Have you heard the latest from the second floor?"

"The boy in the wheelchair?" Sarah asked, pulling her dark hair back into a messy ponytail.

"Yes!" Jessica practically bounced on her mattress, eager to share the gossip. "The RA down the hall was just talking about it. Apparently, it was a massive clerical error, but he actually moved in. He's living in the ADA suite with five girls."

"The what suite?" Sarah asked, her brow furrowing slightly. "Sorry, I'm still learning all your American acronyms."

"Oh, right, ADA," Jessica clarified. "Americans with Disabilities Act. It just means it's the handicap-accessible suite. Like, if you look at the doors for Rooms 214 and 216 downstairs, they have two peepholes—one at standard standing height, and a second one drilled lower so you can see out from a wheelchair—plus motorized door openers and **PUSH TO OPEN** buttons inside. It's the only accessible suite on that side of the building."

"Ah, right. Makes perfect sense," Sarah nodded, the logistics clicking into place for her. "Well, I actually saw them earlier," she mentioned, walking over to the small, single window that looked out over the rolling hills of the campus. "When I was walking over to Kincaid Hall, I saw the whole group heading toward the dining hall."

"And? Is it as weird as everyone says?"

Sarah looked out the window, watching the tiny figures of students navigating the winding paths below. She thought about the boy in the wheelchair, and the way the girls and Hannah's boyfriend had been navigating the campus paths with him. She remembered the fierce, protective bubble that seemed to surround their little group, and the unmistakable look of absolute adoration on the face of the girl walking right beside his chair.

"No," Sarah said softly, turning away from the glass. "It didn't look weird at all. Honestly? It looked like he found exactly where he was supposed to be."

## First Days

### Scene 1: Morning Divergence

**Date:** 2014-08-25 at 07:30 America/New_York

The sharp, mechanical buzz of a digital alarm clock shattered the quiet of Room 214.

Aubrie’s hand shot out from under the blankets, blindly slapping the snooze button on the nightstand to kill the noise. As the room settled back into a heavy silence, Jordan opened his eyes. He was greeted by the steady, reassuring rhythm of Aubrie’s breathing. She was still curled up beside him in the narrow bed, her arm draped protectively across his waist. The nightmare-fueled dread that usually accompanied his mornings was entirely absent, replaced by a profound, grounding safety.

Above them, the springs of the top bunk creaked. Madison swung her legs over the side and climbed down the wooden ladder, her hair sleep-tousled but her energy already shifting into practical motion.

"Alright, suite family," Madison whispered brightly, kneeling beside the dresser to pull out a stack of clean clothes. "First day of classes. Let's get moving."

Aubrie stirred, rubbing the sleep from her eyes. She offered Jordan a soft, lingering smile before untangling herself from the blankets. Without a word of complaint, the two women fell into a seamless, coordinated routine. They gathered Jordan’s clean clothes, a stack of towels, and their own shower caddies, then helped him transfer into his wheelchair.

They bypassed the cramped connecting bathroom and headed down the quiet hallway to the spacious accessible shower room. Once the heavy wooden door clicked shut and locked behind them, the radical, judgment-free logic of their shared living arrangement took over.

There was no hesitation and no performance of modesty. They helped Jordan navigate the difficult process of toileting with clinical, unwavering respect. Then, guided by pure pragmatism, the two women utilized the facilities themselves. They were already there, the morning clock was ticking, and they had already crossed the ultimate threshold of vulnerability with one another. It was a shared, undeniable reality: they all had physical needs, and there was absolutely no reason to hide them.

When it was time to shower, the dynamic shifted from simple caretaking to something deeper. The accessible room featured only a single large, roll-in shower stall. With the clock ticking toward their morning lectures, Aubrie turned on the warm water, letting the stall fill with steam. She looked at Jordan, her cheeks flushing slightly, but her eyes held a steady, undeniable affection.

Instead of reaching in from the outside to assist him, Aubrie stepped fully into the water with him.

It was a quiet, monumental shift. As the warm water washed over them, she carefully washed his hair, her touches lingering, infused with a tender, budding romance that went far beyond mere assistance. Jordan closed his eyes, his heart hammering against his ribs. He had never been touched with such gentleness, never been looked at as someone worthy of romantic intimacy. Having Aubrie pressed close to him in the warm steam was a revelation of absolute trust.

A few minutes later, Aubrie finished washing her own hair. She glanced through the steam at Madison, who was patiently waiting her turn near the sinks. Aubrie realized the math of the morning commute was getting tight, but more importantly, she remembered the profound pact they had made the afternoon prior. Madison fancied him, too.

Aubrie reached for a towel and stepped out of the water, offering Madison a warm, encouraging smile as she handed over the bottle of shampoo.

"I'm all rinsed off, and we're on a massive time crunch," Aubrie said smoothly, her voice completely devoid of jealousy. "Do you want to step in and finish helping him while you wash up?"

Madison’s blue eyes widened in mild surprise for a fraction of a second before softening into a brilliant, deeply grateful smile. "Yeah," Madison said softly, appreciating the beautiful lack of territorial boundaries. "I'd love to."

Madison stepped into the warm stall, taking over the shower. The energy shifted from Aubrie's quiet, romantic intensity to Madison's bright, affectionate warmth. As Madison washed her own hair and expertly helped Jordan finish his shower, she chatted easily with him, her hands gentle and caring. For Jordan, sitting in the warm steam, the experience was earth-shattering. He was being handed back and forth not as a burdensome chore, but as someone cherished. He was experiencing the absolute, undeniable proof of their Constellation.

Once they were all clean, dried, and dressed, they returned to Room 214. Madison immediately opened the connecting bathroom door and knocked lightly on the door to Room 216.

"Are you three decent?" Madison called out.

"We are," Zoë’s crisp British voice replied.

Hannah and Lauren stepped through the bathroom, fully dressed and buzzing with first-day energy. Lauren practically bounced on her heels, eager to go meet Tom before his early lecture, while Hannah gave a warm, contented smile, already anticipating her breakfast with Mark.

"Have a great first day, J," Hannah said, leaning down to wrap her arms securely around Jordan’s shoulders in a tight, sisterly hug.

Lauren followed suit, giving him a cheerful squeeze. "See you guys this afternoon!"

As Hannah and Lauren disappeared out the suite door to meet their boyfriends, Jordan watched them go. He realized, with a wave of calm clarity, that he didn't feel abandoned. He wasn't being left behind. He was simply watching his family expand their perimeter.

"Right then," Zoë said, adjusting her glasses as she walked into Room 214, her backpack slung over one shoulder. "Shall we proceed to the dining hall? I require a proper cup of tea before I can process any academic syllabi, though I am fully bracing myself for the American atrocity of lukewarm water and paper-flavored tea."

The four of them made the trek across the rolling campus to Kincaid Hall. The cafeteria was already a chaotic sea of freshmen scrambling to eat before their morning lectures.

While Aubrie and Madison guided Jordan toward the food stations, Zoë broke off toward the beverage line with low expectations. She anticipated finding a standard metal urn dispensing tepid water. Instead, she stopped dead in her tracks.

Mounted directly into the stainless-steel counter was a dedicated, high-pressure boiling water tap. A small digital readout on the dispenser proudly displayed **100°C**. Beside it sat a neatly organized rack of premium, robust black tea bags.

"Can I help you find anything, hon?" a cafeteria worker asked, noticing Zoë staring at the tap.

"The water," Zoë said, pointing to the readout. "It is actually boiling. Not simmering. Boiling."

The worker laughed, wiping down the counter. "Oh, yeah. CPI has a massive British exchange program. Five years ago, they nearly started a riot over the coffee urns. The university finally ripped out the old machines and installed proper boiling taps in every dining hall on campus just to keep the peace."

A rare, thoroughly impressed smile spread across Zoë's face. "Outstanding."

She prepared her tea exactly to her specifications—steeping the bag in the boiling water for precisely four minutes before adding a splash of milk—and rejoined the group with a newfound respect for the university's logistics.

She found them in the serving area, joining the mass of students staring up at the brightly illuminated breakfast menus. Jordan wheeled forward, immediately hunching his shoulders. He squinted intensely at the glowing boards, his eyes narrowing into slits as he tried to force the blurry, floating shapes into legible words.

Before Aubrie could even take a step forward, Zoë moved seamlessly to his side, cradling her perfectly brewed tea in one hand.

Zoë gently wrapped her free arm around Jordan's shoulders, pulling him into a secure, grounding side-hug. Leaning down, she cupped her hand around his right ear, expertly blocking out the overwhelming wall of cafeteria noise.

"The font scaling on these displays is highly illogical," Zoë murmured directly into his ear, her crisp London accent cutting through the static with perfect clarity. "Let us review your options. Station one offers scrambled eggs, bacon, and toast. Station two is a waffle bar. Which data set appeals to you this morning?"
