"Welcome to Health Sciences 201: Dimensions of Human Sexuality," she announced, her voice echoing clearly through the massive room. Instantly, the same audio pumped directly into Jordan's headphones, crisp and perfectly isolated from the background noise. "I am Dr. Evans."

The projector flared to life, casting the syllabus onto the massive screen at the front of the room. A split second later, the exact same document appeared in high resolution on Jordan's silver mPad.

"Over the next fifteen weeks, we are going to have frank, mature, and clinical discussions about human biology," Dr. Evans continued, clicking to the first slide. "We will cover male and female anatomical structures, the physiological mechanics of the reproductive system, physical response cycles, and sexual health. And let me be clear right now: I expect you to know the proper medical terminology for the exam. But I also know what you all call these parts on the weekend."

A ripple of nervous laughter moved through the auditorium.

"I am not afraid to use the street slang in this room," she stated flatly, pacing the stage. "If we are talking about a penis or a vagina, we will use the clinical terms, but I will also make sure you understand the slang you're going to hear out in the real world. There is zero shame in this classroom. Anatomy is anatomy."

Lauren and Sarah exchanged a highly amused glance. Mark let out a quiet chuckle. Aubrie and Madison pulled out their notebooks, immediately respecting the professor's unapologetic vibe.

"A few ground rules," Dr. Evans said, leaning against the front podium. "First: my office is in Room 412. There is a massive glass bowl of condoms sitting right on the edge of my desk. They are free. You do not need to ask me for them. You do not need to make eye contact with me. Just walk in, take what you need, and walk out. Second: this class covers heavy, deeply personal material. If any of you need to have a private conversation with me—whether you have questions about birth control, if you need resources for an abortion, or if you simply grew up incredibly sheltered and need extra help understanding the basics without the whole class staring at you—email me. We will meet one-on-one outside of my regular office hours. Total privacy. No judgment, ever."

The room grew quiet, a genuine wave of respect washing over the three hundred students.

"Finally," Dr. Evans said, clapping her hands together once. "Before we dive into chapter one, I have a standard request from Educational Accessibility. We have a student in this section who requires a peer note-taker. The university will compensate you with Priority Course Registration. If you have decent handwriting and want the perk, come see me at the podium after class."

Down in the accessible row, all nine of Jordan's friends—Aubrie, Madison, Zoë, Lauren, Hannah, Mark, Sarah, Jessica, and Chloe—simultaneously turned their heads to look at him, grinning. Jordan couldn't help but smile, his cheeks flushing slightly at his own private army of note-takers.

But as Dr. Evans clicked to the next slide, launching into the introductory material, Jordan's smile quickly faded.

He was staring down at his mPad. The screen displayed a list of the upcoming textbook chapters. He read the clinical terminology, his eyes scanning the incredibly detailed anatomical diagrams that served as the header for the document. Dr. Evans fired off a few of the slang terms alongside the medical ones, but it didn't help.

A cold, heavy knot of pure panic began to form in his stomach.

He didn't know what any of these words meant. He didn't understand the diagrams. He didn't even know the slang. His mother hadn't just opted him out of the classes; she had actively shamed the concept of the human body to the point where he had learned to completely ignore his own physical existence. While the rest of the three hundred students in the room were comfortably absorbing the lecture, Jordan was staring at a completely alien language.

He looked down at his own hands, resting on the armrests of his wheelchair, a sudden, overwhelming wave of inadequacy washing over him. He wasn't just behind. He was entirely, fundamentally lost.

### Scene 2: Office Hours

**Date:** 2014-08-26 at 10:50 America/New_York

At ten-fifty, the massive lecture hall erupted into the chaotic sounds of three hundred freshmen packing up their bags. As the crowd funneled up the tiered stairs toward the exits, Jordan’s group stayed exactly where they were.

Jordan carefully closed the lid of his Vanguard Pro laptop, sliding it into his backpack alongside his new mPad and FM receiver. Once the room had mostly cleared out, Aubrie unlocked the brakes on his wheelchair, and the massive ten-person entourage made its way down to the front of the stage.

Dr. Evans was at the podium, clicking out of her presentation. She looked up, her eyebrows raised in mild surprise as an absolute wall of students approached her.

"Well," Dr. Evans said, leaning against the podium with an amused smile. "I usually have to beg for a single peer note-taker. I don't think I've ever had an entire platoon volunteer on the first day."

Sarah laughed, stepping forward. "We're a package deal, Professor."

"And which one of you is the student registered with Educational Accessibility?" Dr. Evans asked, looking over the group.

Jordan raised his hand slightly, offering a shy, polite smile.

Dr. Evans looked at Jordan, then back at the nine people standing defensively and affectionately around his wheelchair. "Let me guess. You guys all live on the same floor?"

"Same suite, actually," Madison grinned. "Well, five of us. Plus a boyfriend, and three friends from the fifth floor. Have you heard the rumor about the housing clerical error in Bennett Hall?"

Dr. Evans’ eyes widened in sudden realization. She let out a sharp, genuine laugh. "Wait. You're the Suite 214 kid? The one who got dropped into a room full of women?"

Jordan nodded, pulling his laptop back out of his bag for a second just to type a quick response. He turned the screen so she could read the glowing green text.

<aac>I am. They decided to keep me.</aac>

"I can see that," Dr. Evans smiled, her tone softening with genuine warmth. "Alright, tell you what. My office hours start in ten minutes. Why don't you all follow me up to Room 412, and we'll get everyone's student ID numbers written down. If you're all willing to share the note-taking load for Jordan, I am more than happy to authorize Priority Registration for the whole group."

A collective cheer went up from the freshmen.

They followed Dr. Evans out of the auditorium and took the massive freight elevator up to the fourth floor. Room 412 was a standard, slightly cramped faculty office, filled with towering bookshelves and the smell of old paper. Sitting right on the edge of her desk, exactly as promised, was a massive, crystal-clear glass bowl overflowing with brightly colored foil condom wrappers.

It was a tight squeeze getting ten students into the office, but they made it work, shuffling around Jordan's wheelchair. Dr. Evans pulled out a legal pad and passed it around, letting Mark, Sarah, Jessica, Chloe, Hannah, Lauren, Zoë, Madison, and Aubrie write down their names and CPI ID numbers.

While the pad made its rounds, Jordan opened his Vanguard Pro again. His fingers hovered over the keyboard. The panic he had felt during the lecture was still sitting heavy in his chest, but Dr. Evans’ announcement at the beginning of class—her promise of total privacy and zero judgment—gave him the courage he needed.

He took a deep breath and began to type.

When the legal pad finally made its way back to Dr. Evans, Jordan pushed his wheelchair forward slightly, turning his laptop screen toward her desk.

Dr. Evans adjusted her glasses and read the bright green text.

<aac>Professor Evans, you said to let you know if we grew up extremely sheltered. My mother opted me out of every Family Life and health class since the fourth grade. She told me the human body was shameful. I don't know what any of the diagrams on the syllabus mean. I don't know any of the slang. I don't know how any of this works. I am completely lost.</aac>

The easy, amused energy in the office instantly vanished. Dr. Evans read the paragraph twice. When she looked up at Jordan, her eyes were filled with profound, quiet compassion.

Around him, the suite fell completely silent. Aubrie stepped a little closer, resting her hand gently on the back of his wheelchair in a silent show of absolute support. Lauren felt a sharp pang of empathy in her chest, the contrast between Jordan's deeply vulnerable honesty and Tom's toxic paranoia striking her all over again.

"Jordan," Dr. Evans said softly, her voice entirely devoid of pity, replaced instead with a fierce, protective academic respect. "Thank you for telling me that. I know that took a lot of courage."

She turned to her own desktop computer, her fingers flying across the keyboard.

"You are not the first student to sit in that chair with this exact problem," she told him firmly. "When parents completely isolate their children from basic biology, it does real damage. But you are here now, and we are going to fix it. There is absolutely nothing shameful about the human body, and you are not going to fail this class."

She hit print, and the machine in the corner of her office whirred to life. She grabbed the pages and handed them to Jordan. It was a list of highly reputable, clinical web resources—university health sites and basic anatomical guides designed for young adults.

"Start here," Dr. Evans instructed gently. "Look over these sites at your own pace. They explain the absolute basics. Read them over the next few days. If you are still feeling lost, you email me, and we will sit down one-on-one, and I will explain whatever you need. Okay?"

Jordan looked down at the printed URLs, a massive wave of relief washing over him. He looked back up, nodded emphatically, and typed: <aac>Thank you.</aac>

Aubrie leaned over Jordan's shoulder, looking at the printed pages. She saw the URLs for the 2D diagrams and clinical explanations. It was a great start, but she knew Jordan. She knew how his brain worked, and she knew how deeply ingrained his mother's psychological conditioning was. Staring at flat, sterile drawings on a computer screen wasn't going to break eighteen years of trauma.

Aubrie slowly looked up, catching Madison's eye across the cramped office.

Madison, who was looking at the exact same printout, raised an eyebrow. An unspoken, radical, and intensely practical idea passed between the two girls. They had built an ecosystem of absolute trust in Room 214. They had already broken down so many physical barriers just by sharing a bathroom and a sleeping space.

Aubrie looked back down at Jordan, squeezing his shoulder.

"Don't worry, J," Aubrie said softly, a warm, protective promise in her voice. "We'll go over the printouts with you. We'll figure out a way to help you study tonight when we get back to the dorm. I promise."

Dr. Evans smiled, completely unaware of the unconventional lengths the girls of Suite 214 were willing to go to for their friend. "You've got a great support system here, Jordan. I'll see you all on Thursday."

### Scene 3: The Mega-Group Lunch

**Date:** 2014-08-26 at 12:15 America/New_York

By the time the massive ten-person entourage made it to Kincaid Commons, the lunchtime rush was in full swing. It took some serious maneuvering, but Mark and Sarah managed to push three rectangular tables together near the back windows, creating enough space for everyone, including a spot at the head of the table for Jordan’s wheelchair.

The energy was loud, chaotic, and incredibly warm. They traded stories about their bizarre first-day professors, the dreadful dining hall pizza, and the absolute absurdity of their shared health class.

Sitting across from each other, Lauren and Sarah naturally gravitated toward a side conversation. Despite growing up worlds apart—Lauren from Canada and Sarah from the bustling coast of Sydney—they hit it off instantly. Sarah was drawn to Lauren's gentle, steady kindness, while Lauren found Sarah's bold, unapologetic confidence incredibly refreshing.

"I still can't believe you managed to get a job at the campus coffee shop as a freshman," Lauren said, taking a sip of her water. "I tried to apply, and they practically laughed me out of the student union."

"You just have to be incredibly persistent," Sarah grinned, stealing a fry from Jessica's plate. "Or just have an accent. I swear the manager only hired me because he thinks Americans like ordering lattes from Australians."

As they laughed, Lauren’s mPhone, resting face-up on the table, buzzed loudly against the laminate wood.

The screen lit up with a new text message. The contact name simply read: **My MVP 🥍**

Lauren’s smile instantly faltered. Her stomach gave that same, uneasy flutter she had felt on the top bunk the night before. She didn't open the message; she just quickly reached out and flipped the phone face-down.

Directly across the table, Sarah’s phone also vibrated. Sarah glanced down at her screen. The contact name read: **Lax Toss 🙄**

Sarah let out a quiet, frustrated sigh, rolling her eyes as she hit the lock button to silence it and shoved the phone into her backpack. She looked up and caught Lauren's eye.

"Boy drama?" Sarah asked sympathetically, nodding toward Lauren’s face-down phone.

"Something like that," Lauren deflected with a tight, forced smile. "Just... a lot of texts today."

"I hear you. Mine's being incredibly aggro this week," Sarah commiserated, waving a hand dismissively. "It's exhausting. But anyway, what's your major? You seem like an English girl to me."

Lauren visibly relaxed, grateful for the pivot. "Nailed it. English literature."

They smoothly transitioned back into the safety of academic small talk, neither of them mentioning a name, entirely unaware that the same aggressively insecure lacrosse player was currently sitting in his room on the other side of campus, glaring at his phone and wondering why neither of his girlfriends was answering him.

By a quarter to one, the dynamic of the table began to shift as the reality of afternoon classes set in.

"Alright, team," Mark announced, standing up and grabbing his tray. "I have a two-hour chemistry lab on the other side of the quad, so I need to run."

"Same," Hannah said, standing up to join him. "And Zoë and I have a seminar at one."

Sarah checked her watch and groaned. "Jessica and Chloe and I have a mandatory freshman orientation module in ten minutes. We have to bolt." Sarah slung her backpack over her shoulder, turning to Lauren. "It was so great meeting you. We all need to get together for dinner tonight. Same tables?"

"Absolutely," Lauren smiled. "See you tonight."

Within a minute, the chaotic energy of the mega-group dispersed, leaving only Jordan, Aubrie, Madison, and Lauren sitting at the long table.

With the loud buffer of the group gone, the silence felt a little heavier. Lauren looked down at her dark phone. She knew there were at least five unread messages waiting for her, and she felt physically sick at the thought of reading them.

Aubrie leaned forward, her elbows on the table, studying Lauren’s face. The protective instincts she usually reserved for Jordan immediately extended across the table.

"Lauren," Aubrie said softly, cutting right to the chase. "Are you okay? You've been looking at your phone like it's a bomb all through lunch."

Lauren let out a shaky breath, tracing the edge of her phone case. The absolute trust that defined Suite 214/216 made it impossible to lie to them.

"It's my boyfriend," Lauren admitted quietly, her voice barely carrying over the dining hall chatter. "We were on the phone last night while you guys were showing Jordan the Dark Aero desktop. I... I told him about Jordan."

Jordan, who had his silver mPad resting on his lap, looked up, his eyes wide with quiet concern.

"He didn't take it well," Lauren continued, her voice thick with unshed frustration. "He didn't just think it was weird. He immediately jumped to the worst, most disgusting conclusion possible. He accused Jordan of playing a pity card to try and sleep with all of us. He called our suite a 'messed-up thing'."

Madison's jaw dropped. "Are you kidding me?"

"I wish I was," Lauren whispered, blinking back a sudden wave of tears. "And now he's blowing up my phone because I haven't answered him since nine-thirty this morning. He's demanding to know where I am and if I'm with 'the wheelchair guy'. He's just so... angry. And controlling. And I'm just sitting here realizing that I'm not happy. I haven't been happy for a while."

She looked up at the three of them, terrified that she was bringing too much drama into their peaceful dynamic.

Instead of judgment, Jordan reached across the table. He didn't type anything. He just placed his hand gently over hers, a quiet, physical anchor of solidarity.

"Lauren," Madison said fiercely, reaching out to squeeze her other arm. "He does not get to talk to you like that, and he certainly doesn't get to talk about our family like that. Mark told you last night, and we are telling you right now: we have your back."

"You don't have to put up with him just because you're used to him," Aubrie added, her voice steady and fiercely protective. "Whatever you decide to do, we are right here."

Lauren looked at the three of them. She had known them for barely four days, but the safety she felt in this exact moment was more profound than anything she had felt in months of dating Tom.

"Thank you," Lauren breathed, a genuine, tearful smile breaking through the anxiety.

Aubrie stood up and walked around the table, pulling Lauren out of her chair. Madison immediately joined them, wrapping her arms around Lauren's shoulders. Jordan locked the wheels of his chair and leaned forward, wrapping his arms securely around Lauren's waist to complete the circle.

It was a tight, solid, profoundly grounding group hug. For Jordan, physical touch was no longer something to flinch away from; it was how his family communicated safety.

"Okay," Lauren whispered, wiping her eyes as they finally pulled back. "I have a two o'clock literature lecture. I'll be okay. I promise."

"We'll see you back at the suite before dinner," Aubrie smiled, unlocking Jordan's brakes. "You're safe with us, Lauren. Always."

### Scene 4: The Fifth Floor Hunch

**Date:** 2014-08-26 at 15:15 America/New_York

The air conditioning on the fifth floor of Bennett Hall was notoriously erratic. While Suite 214/216 downstairs was perfectly comfortable, Room 505 was currently functioning like a walk-in freezer.

Sarah Braithwaite was sitting cross-legged on her bed, wrapped in a thick fleece blanket, staring at the glowing screen of her mPhone. On the floor, Jessica and Chloe were organizing their binders from the afternoon orientation module. The connecting bathroom door was propped wide open, and their three suitemates from Room 507—Ashley, Emily, and Cassie—were casually drifting between the two rooms, sharing a giant bag of popcorn.

Sarah’s phone vibrated against her knee for what felt like the dozenth time in the last two hours.

She unlocked the screen. It was another barrage of texts from **Lax Toss 🙄**.

<sms>Where are you? You said your orientation ended at 2. It's 3:15. Are you hanging out in the lobby? Who are you with?</sms>

Sarah let out a heavy, exhausted sigh, locking the screen and tossing the phone onto her pillow.

"That's it," Sarah announced to the room, her Australian accent clipping her words with finality. "I'm tossing him. I am deadset done."

Chloe looked up from her binder, her eyes widening. "Wait, you're breaking up with Tom?"

"Who else?" Sarah groaned, falling backward onto her mattress and staring at the cinderblock ceiling. "He is being so incredibly aggro today. It's like a switch flipped last night. He's demanding to know exactly where I am, who I'm with, why I didn't text him back during lunch... it's exhausting. He's acting like a complete psycho."

Cassie wandered in from the bathroom connecting Room 507, popping a piece of popcorn into her mouth. "Honestly? Good for you. We didn't want to say anything since you just got here, but he always gave me weird vibes. Way too intense."

"He really is," Emily agreed, leaning against the doorframe next to Ashley. "You're gorgeous, Sarah, and you're hilarious. You don't need a guy who texts you like a parole officer."

"Exactly," Ashley nodded enthusiastically. "We fully support this. You deserve way better."

Sarah smiled up at the ceiling, genuinely touched by the immediate, unquestioning loyalty of her suitemates. It was exactly what she needed to hear to solidify her decision.

Jessica, however, was frowning thoughtfully, tapping her pen against her notebook. "Mate, he's been acting like a bit of a tosser all week. But today is definitely next level. You don't reckon it has something to do with that co-ed suite downstairs, do you?"

Sarah sat up, pulling the blanket around her shoulders. "What? Jordan? Tom doesn't even know I'm friends with Jordan. I haven't mentioned him once."

"Yeah, but Bennett Hall is a mixed building," Chloe pointed out, connecting the dots. "There are guys' suites on every floor—I mean, Mark and his suitemates are right down on the third floor. But a guy actually living inside a girls' suite? That rumor is literally the biggest thing on campus right now."

"Exactly," Jessica said, pointing her pen at Sarah. "Maybe Tom heard the rumors and is getting weirdly possessive. He knows you're friendly, and he knows you live here. Maybe he's paranoid you're hanging out in some giant 'love shack' down on the second floor with the bloke everyone is talking about."

It was a perfectly logical hunch. From their perspective, Tom's sudden, frantic obsession with Sarah's whereabouts seemed like toxic jealousy over the dorm's most famous resident.

None of them knew the actual truth. None of them knew that Tom’s frantic texting had absolutely nothing to do with Jordan, and everything to do with the fact that his other girlfriend, Lauren, lived in Room 216. The walls of Tom's carefully constructed, two-timing reality were closing in on him. The sheer, terrifying proximity of his two relationships existing inside the exact same cinderblock building was pushing him into a state of absolute, paranoid panic. He was terrified they were going to run into each other in the lobby, or the laundry room, or at Kincaid Commons.

"Even if he did think that, it's completely pathetic," Sarah said, shaking her head. "Jordan is the sweetest, most respectful guy I've met here. If Tom is that deeply insecure about a bloke in a wheelchair just existing near me, then he's even more cooked than I thought."

Sarah reached over and picked her phone back up, silencing the notifications entirely.

"It honestly feels like he's projecting," Sarah muttered, looking at the dark screen. "He's acting like he's terrified I'm going to find out some massive, horrible secret, and he's taking all that paranoid guilt out on me. I'm over it. I'm going to cut him loose."

"Good," Jessica said firmly, giving Sarah a supportive nod. "If he chucks a wobbly about it, tell him he can take it up with the five of us."

"Make that six," Chloe corrected, grinning.

Sarah laughed, the heavy weight of the toxic relationship finally starting to lift off her shoulders. She didn't know exactly when or how she was going to end it, but as she looked around the cramped, freezing dorm room at her five suitemates from Suite 505/507, she felt perfectly secure in her decision.

### Scene 5: An Unconventional Study Session

**Date:** 2014-08-26 at 20:15 America/New_York

That Tuesday evening, after their first full day of classes, the group was relaxing in the suite. The health class, however, was still heavily on Jordan's mind. He wheeled himself over to where the girls were gathered in Room 214, holding his *Dimensions of Human Sexuality* textbook and syllabus like a mysterious, terrifying map.

He opened his Vanguard Pro laptop, the neon-green text glowing against the sleek interface as he typed a confession that mirrored what he had told Dr. Evans earlier that day.

<aac>That health class today,</aac> he typed, his words glowing with hesitant but earnest vulnerability. <aac>All those chapter titles... about the human body... I've never learned about any of that. My mother forcibly opted me out of those classes every year. She made me feel like it was dirty to even ask.</aac>

A deep, collective empathy filled the room. They understood this wasn't just about academics; it was about a fundamental part of his education and his own humanity that had been cruelly denied to him. It was Aubrie who, looking at the confusing, sterile diagrams in the open textbook on her desk, formulated a plan rooted in the unique trust their family had built.

"The diagrams in the book are just flat drawings, Jordan. It's hard to learn from that," she said matter-of-factly. "We could... help you. It would be easier to remember if you could connect the clinical terms to physical reality."

The offer was radical, generous, and born of pure, practical kindness. Zoë and Madison nodded in immediate agreement, their faces completely devoid of judgment.

Hannah and Lauren, out of deep respect for their own boyfriends, lovingly opted out. "That is an incredibly sweet and amazing thing to do for him," Hannah said, offering a supportive smile. "Just knock on the connecting door when you guys are done." With that, the two of them retreated through the bathroom to Room 216, pulling the door shut behind them until it clicked, giving the others total privacy.

Jordan looked at the three remaining women, a mix of nervousness and profound gratitude on his face. Aubrie gave him a reassuring smile.

"Ready for your first real health class?" Aubrie asked.

Zoë walked over and double-checked that the heavy hallway door was pulled firmly shut and its automatic electronic lock was engaged. Madison reached up and flicked off the harsh, fluorescent overhead lights, leaving the room bathed only in the warm, gentle amber glow of their desk lamps. The atmosphere transformed instantly. It wasn't a party; it was a sanctuary.

Aubrie sat on the edge of her bed, patting the mattress. Madison and Zoë sat on either side of her, holding the open textbook. Before anything else happened, Aubrie looked Jordan directly in the eyes, setting the absolute boundaries of the room.

"Jordan, listen to me," Aubrie said softly, her voice steady and anchoring. "Here is what to expect. This isn't scary. There is absolutely no pressure. We are in complete control of this environment, and if you get overwhelmed, or if you just get cold feet, we stop immediately. No questions asked. Do you understand?"

Jordan let out a shaky breath, the last remnants of his anxiety melting away. He nodded. He trusted them with his life.

What followed was the quiet, methodical dismantling of a lifetime of trauma. The study session was clinical, gentle, and profoundly intimate, completely devoid of the shame his mother had weaponized against him. Using the textbook as a guide, Aubrie, Madison, and Zoë patiently helped Jordan map the sterile, confusing medical terminology to real, physical bodies.

They replaced the taboo with simple, practical truth.

As the quiet reassurances filled the room, accompanied by the soft turning of textbook pages, a massive psychological weight lifted off Jordan’s shoulders. He realized, with overwhelming clarity, that the human body wasn't filthy. It wasn't something to be hidden or despised. The contrast between the cold medical diagrams and the warmth, trust, and fierce protectiveness of his suitemates broke the final, lingering hold of his mother's Puritanical control.

Because they offered him such radical vulnerability, Jordan felt an unprecedented surge of empowerment. For the first time in his life, he didn't feel broken or ashamed of his own form. He willingly returned the trust, allowing them to map his anatomy clinically alongside the textbook chapters, realizing that his body was just as natural and deserving of care as theirs.

By the time the session ended, the lingering ghosts of his mother's psychological prison had been entirely evicted from Room 214.

They all changed into their soft cotton pajamas. Jordan sat in his wheelchair near his desk, feeling a deep, resounding peace settle into his bones. He wasn't lost anymore. He was whole, educated, and completely safe. A soft, confident smile rested on his face as he watched Aubrie, Madison, and Zoë chat quietly on the beds.

### Scene 6: The Kincaid Hall Collision

**Date:** 2014-08-26 at 20:25 America/New_York

Moments earlier, when Hannah and Lauren had stepped out of Room 214 to give Jordan, Aubrie, Madison, and Zoë privacy for their study session, Lauren had followed Hannah through the connecting bathroom and back into Room 216. Mark was already sitting at Hannah's desk, packing up his Political Science notes.

Lauren shut the bathroom door behind them, ensuring it clicked to give the others total privacy. But as she turned around to face the cramped room, the heavy, suffocating dread that had been building in her chest all day finally boiled over. Her phone was still vibrating in her pocket with Tom's toxic demands.

"I can't just sit here and wait for him to blow up again," Lauren blurted out, her voice shaking with a mix of anxiety and sudden, undeniable resolve. "I need to go to Kincaid Hall. He texted that he's hanging out at the student center lounge, and I need to look him in the eye and ask him point-blank what is going on with him."

Hannah immediately grabbed her lanyard off her desk, making sure her Poly Pass was attached. "We're going with you."

"Damn right we are," Mark agreed, slinging his backpack over his shoulder. He stepped up next to Lauren, offering a solid, protective presence. "He's not pulling this aggressive garbage on you alone."

The three of them left Bennett Hall, stepping out into the warm, humid Virginia evening. They walked briskly across the brick pathways of the campus quad, the glowing lampposts casting long shadows.

As they neared the massive brick-and-limestone footprint of Kincaid Hall, the campus student center, they heard a chorus of distinctly female voices approaching from the other side of the walkway. Lauren looked up to see a formidable squad of six girls marching with absolute, synchronized purpose.

At the front of the pack was Sarah Braithwaite.

"Sarah?" Lauren called out, genuinely surprised to see her new friend from Dr. Evans' lecture hall.

Sarah stopped, her suitemates from Suite 505/507 fanning out behind her like a protective wall.

As the fifth-floor group spread out under the glowing lampposts, Lauren, Hannah, and Mark all stopped dead in their tracks. It was their very first time seeing all six of the fifth-floor suitemates standing together, and their eyes instantly locked onto the woman standing directly at Sarah's right shoulder.

Jessica Gallagher stood at a staggering 6'3"—190.5 centimeters—her heavily muscled, natural bodybuilder physique practically eclipsing the rest of the group. Dressed for pure, unbothered utility, her sheer size and unyielding posture radiated a cold, absolute presence. Mark, despite his own solid, broad-shouldered 5'8" frame, found himself having to crane his neck upward just to meet her piercing blue eyes, while Lauren and Hannah simply stared in mild, intimidated awe.

"Lauren! Hey. What are you guys doing out here?" Sarah asked, completely oblivious to the stunned silence her towering roommate was currently commanding.

Lauren blinked, forcing herself to snap out of her shock and focus back on Sarah. "I'm heading to the student center," Lauren admitted, gesturing vaguely toward the brightly lit windows of Kincaid Hall ahead of them. She offered a tight, self-deprecating smile. "My boyfriend is being incredibly controlling and paranoid today. He's hanging out by the pool tables, and I'm going to confront him. Mark and Hannah are my backup."

Sarah’s eyes lit up with fierce, instantaneous solidarity. "You're kidding. I'm literally on my way to the Kincaid Hall pool tables right now to dump my boyfriend for doing the exact same thing! He's been an absolute psycho all day."

"Wait, really?" Lauren breathed, feeling a sudden surge of comfort in the sheer coincidence.

"Deadset," Sarah nodded, gesturing to the giant standing beside her, and then to the rest of the girls from Rooms 505 and 507: Chloe, Ashley, Emily, and Cassie. "I brought the whole fifth-floor cavalry with me. There is strength in numbers, mate. Come on, let's go together. We'll dump our toxic boyfriends and then all go get ice cream."

Lauren actually laughed, the heavy anxiety in her chest lifting slightly. Having Mark and Hannah as backup was great, but having the towering, heavily muscled Australian woman as part of their combined army suddenly made the impending confrontation feel like a guaranteed victory.

"Deal," Lauren said.

The combined group of nine freshmen—a veritable army of supportive friends—marched through the glass double doors of Kincaid Hall.

As they approached the felt billiard tables in the student lounge, Lauren scanned the crowd. "He said he was wearing his lacrosse hoodie..."

"Mine plays lacrosse too," Sarah muttered, her eyes narrowing as she looked past a group of upperclassmen. "There he is."

Lauren followed Sarah's gaze. Her heart dropped into her stomach. "Wait. That's my boyfriend."

Sarah stopped walking. She looked at Lauren, her brow furrowing in profound confusion. "What? No, Lauren, that's my boyfriend."

A terrifying, suffocating silence fell over the two girls amid the chaotic noise of the student center. Behind them, Mark's jaw tightened, while Jessica and Chloe exchanged a horrified look as the math suddenly, violently clicked into place.

Tom had just lined up a shot on the corner pocket. He looked up, the pool cue still resting in his hands, and saw the massive group approaching.

He saw Sarah. He saw Lauren. He saw them standing shoulder-to-shoulder, staring directly at him, completely surrounded by a protective wall of seven other people.

Tom stopped dead in his tracks. The blood completely drained from his face. The pool cue slipped from his grip, clattering loudly against the floor. His confident, aggressive posture shattered instantly, his shoulders dropping as his brain completely short-circuited. He looked at Sarah, then at Lauren, his mouth opening and closing silently.

"Tom?" Lauren whispered, the betrayal hitting her like a physical blow to the chest. Her eyes instantly filled with tears as the sheer, calculated cruelty of his paranoia finally made sense. "Are you... are you kidding me?"

"Tom!?" Sarah yelled, her Australian accent echoing sharply over the ambient noise of the lounge, causing half the room to turn and look. "Are you having a bloody laugh right now!?"

"Lauren, Sarah, wait—" Tom stammered, taking a panicked step back, his hands raised defensively. "It's a misunderstanding. I can explain—"

"Explain what?!" Sarah exploded, stepping forward. Lauren didn't shrink away; she stepped right up beside Sarah, her sadness instantly hardening into sheer, protective fury.

"This is why you were acting like a psycho!" Lauren realized, her voice trembling with anger. "You weren't worried about the guy in my suite! You were terrified that I was going to talk to the girls in my building and figure out you were playing us both!"

"You projected your own guilty, toxic garbage onto a bloke in a wheelchair!" Sarah shouted, jabbing a finger at his chest. "You are absolute trash, Tom! We are both entirely done with you!"

Tom’s face flushed a deep, panicked crimson. Relying on the only defense mechanism he knew, the 5'2" lacrosse player desperately puffed out his chest, stiffening his posture to try and reclaim his theatrical dominance. He opened his mouth to shout back, preparing to use volume to mask his staggering cowardice.

He never got the chance.

The crowd of friends parted, and Jessica stepped directly into the front line. She possessed a massive threshold for discomfort and never lost her cool over minor inconveniences, but Tom had just crossed a hard moral boundary by acting as a predator targeting someone vulnerable. Her temper instantly ignited.

At an imposing 190.5 cm—or 6'3"—Jessica did not just step up; when she stood up, she took up the entire room. With her natural, heavy-muscled bodybuilder physique forged in the blistering heat of the Outback, she eclipsed the overhead lights. Because she was built like a tank, she didn't need to throw a punch; she relied entirely on physical presence over violence. She marched right up to the edge of the billiard table, forcing Tom to crane his neck sharply upward just to meet her piercing blue eyes.

Jessica glanced down at the pool cue resting on the floor. Her fingers twitched. She wished, with a burning intensity, that she could reach down, grab that stick, and snap it clean in half right in front of his face—the exact way she and her identical twin sister, Bec, used to effortlessly crush industrial timber back home in Petermann. But her pragmatic justice won out; she didn't hold grudges, she fixed problems. Destroying university property inside the student center would only bring disciplinary action down on her own head.

She didn't need to break anything to break him.

Instead, Jessica weaponized her Outback Temper. It wasn't a short fuse or a screaming, chaotic tantrum. It was cold and absolute—a terrifyingly focused, immovable force. She stared a full thirteen inches down at him.

"You aren't explaining anything," Jessica stated, her voice dropping to an unyielding, deadly serious tone that completely shattered Tom's theatrical momentum. "You're a gutless coward. You're going to shut your mouth, you're going to turn around, and you are never going to speak to either of them again. Are we clear?"

The sheer, overwhelming density of her frame obliterated his bravado. Tom visibly shrank, taking a hurried, trembling step backward, completely suffocated by the reality of her physical presence.

Before Tom could even attempt to recover, Mark stepped forward to flank Jessica, folding his arms across his broad chest.

"I think they made themselves clear, man," Mark said, his voice low and dangerous. "Back off."

Tom didn't utter a single syllable.

*Click. Flash.*

Jessica smoothly raised her mPhone and took a crystal-clear, heavily flashed photo of Tom’s pale, cornered face.

"Got it," Jessica announced flatly, turning her back on him with complete, dismissive finality. Calling out the bad behavior and shutting it down, she immediately pivoted to protecting the victims. "Let's go, ladies. The air in here is completely stuffed."

Lauren and Sarah didn't look back. They turned around, the entire army of friends forming a protective perimeter around them, and marched right back out of Kincaid Hall.

The walk back to Bennett Hall was a blur of adrenaline, tears, and furious, protective chatter. Sarah kept her arm wrapped tightly around Lauren’s shoulders, the two women refusing to blame each other for a single second. The target of their rage was entirely, one-hundred-percent Tom.

By the time they reached the second floor of Bennett Hall, Lauren was crying, the emotional exhaustion of the day finally catching up to her. She didn't want to go to her own room. She wanted the absolute, unconditional safety of the family they had built.

She led the massive group down the hall to the door of Room 214.

Just as they gathered outside the door, footsteps approached from the far end of the corridor. Isabel Dawson, the second-floor Resident Advisor, was just finishing her eight-thirty evening round, her CPI Housing clipboard tucked under her arm and her room key already in her hand as she headed back toward her own door—Room 215, directly across the hall from Room 214.

Isabel stopped short as she took in the scene: nine freshmen clustered outside Room 214, Mark's jaw clenched in protective fury, the towering fifth-floor Australian girl standing like a sentinel, and Lauren Barnes sobbing openly into Sarah Braithwaite's shoulder.

Instantly, every trace of routine administrative posture vanished from Isabel's face, replaced by immediate, genuine concern. Instead of unlocking her own door, she stepped right across the hall to join them.

"Lauren? Sarah?" Isabel asked gently, her voice low and soothing as she rested a comforting hand on Lauren's arm. "Hey, sweetie, breathe. What happened? Are you guys okay?"

Before Lauren could even find her voice through her tears, Hannah stepped forward and knocked gently on the heavy wooden door of Room 214. "Madison? It's Hannah. And Mark. And... well, everyone."

Inside, Madison pressed the square stainless-steel **PUSH TO OPEN** handicap button on the interior wall. With a soft electronic click and the whir of the overhead motor, the lock released and the heavy door swung open automatically on its ten-second timer.

The lighting in the room was dim and calm. The unconventional study session was over. Jordan was sitting in his wheelchair near his desk, looking relaxed and peaceful, a soft, confident smile on his face that hadn't been there an hour ago. Aubrie, Madison, and Zoë were sitting on the beds, wrapped in their pajamas.

They looked up, expecting to see Hannah and Lauren. Instead, they saw ten people crowding the hallway—including their RA—with Lauren sobbing into Sarah Braithwaite’s shoulder.

Aubrie was on her feet in an instant. "What happened?"

### Scene 7: The War Room

**Date:** 2014-08-26 at 20:45 America/New_York

The cramped space of Suite 214/216 was never designed to hold twelve emotional freshmen and their Resident Advisor, but nobody cared about the tight squeeze—least of all Isabel Dawson.

Aubrie and Madison immediately ushered everyone inside. Isabel stepped in last, quietly setting her CPI Housing clipboard face-down on the corner of the dresser as the ten-second timer expired and the motorized arm pulled the hallway door shut and locked it automatically behind her so Lauren wouldn't have to cry in a public corridor. By the letter of the residence hall handbook, thirteen people packed into a suite after eight-thirty at night was probably three different occupancy violations. Isabel didn't care in the slightest. Right now, two girls in her building were hurting, and her only job was to make sure they felt safe.

Aubrie and Madison propped both of the connecting bathroom doors wide open, allowing the massive group to spill over into Room 216, while Isabel slipped into the accessible bathroom for a second, grabbed a fresh box of tissues from the roll-under vanity counter where she had helped Jordan unpack his toiletries on Friday afternoon, and brought it back to the bed.

Lauren Barnes and Sarah sat side-by-side on the edge of Jordan’s Twin XL bed. Isabel knelt right on the linoleum floor in front of them, handing tissues to both girls and resting a warm, steady hand over Lauren's trembling knee as the two freshmen explained what had just happened under the bright lights of the Kincaid Hall pool tables.

When Lauren finally dropped the bombshell—*Tom was playing us both*—the reaction from the core suite was immediate. Madison let out a furious gasp, Zoë crossed her arms with a deadly glare, and Aubrie looked like she was ready to march to the student center and dismantle Tom herself. Down on one knee in front of the bed, Isabel closed her eyes for a brief second, a flash of quiet, protective anger crossing her face before she squeezed Lauren's hand even tighter.

"I can't believe he lied to my face," Sarah said, her voice tight with anger as she wiped a stray tear from her cheek. "All day, he was texting me like a paranoid lunatic, making me feel like I was doing something wrong."

Lauren suddenly sat up straighter, the final puzzle piece snapping violently into place.

"That's why he exploded on the phone last night," Lauren realized, her eyes wide as she looked at Aubrie, Madison, and Isabel. "It had absolutely nothing to do with Jordan. It was about the dorm! He realized that I lived on the second floor, and Sarah lived on the fifth floor. He was terrified of the Bennett Hall network. He was trying to isolate me so I wouldn't talk to any of the other girls in the building and figure out his secret!"

"He projected his own guilty garbage onto Jordan," Sarah agreed fiercely, grabbing Lauren's hand. "He is absolute trash, Lauren. You did nothing wrong. Neither of us did."

There was zero friendly fire. The shared trauma of the betrayal had instantly bonded them into a fierce sisterhood. All of their anger was directed entirely, one-hundred-percent at Tom.

"Sarah is completely right, Lauren," Isabel said softly, looking up at both of them with deep, unwavering compassion. "Listen to me, both of you. Guys who manipulate like that always try to make you feel crazy or guilty so you won't compare notes. None of this is on you. I am so sorry he put you through this—and I am so proud of both of you for walking out of there together."

While the room buzzed with protective solidarity, Jordan quietly unlocked his Vanguard Pro. He typed a paragraph, the keyboard clicking softly, and then gently pushed the laptop forward so the screen rested on the mattress between Lauren and Sarah.

<aac>I know exactly what it feels like to be controlled by someone who claims to care about you,</aac> the glowing green text read. <aac>I know what it's like when they try to isolate you so they can keep their power. Tom's toxicity is a reflection of his own brokenness, Lauren. It has nothing to do with you. And he has no power here. Room 214 is a safe haven. For both of you. Always.</aac>

Lauren read the words, a fresh wave of tears hitting her, but this time, they were tears of profound relief. She reached out and hugged Jordan tightly around his shoulders. Sarah read the screen next, her expression softening into deep, respectful awe at the quiet boy in the wheelchair.

Kneeling beside the bed, Isabel read Jordan's screen too. Her throat tightened with emotion as she looked at him. Just four days ago, on Friday afternoon, she had walked into this exact room to find a terrified, isolated boy struggling to plug a frayed laptop cord into an unreachable outlet, too scared to ask for help. Now, surrounded by the women who had refused to let him go, Jordan was the calm, beating heart of the most extraordinary support network she had ever seen in her three years at CPI. If anyone in the Housing Office ever dared question whether the Room 214/216 waiver was working, Isabel was ready to fight them herself.

"Jordan is right," Mark said from the doorway of the bathroom, his voice steady and serious. "Tom just got humiliated by nine people in the middle of Kincaid Hall, and Jessica has a photo of it. He's going to spiral. But he's not getting anywhere near you guys. I'll text my suitemates on the third floor. We'll be your early warning system if he or any of his lacrosse buddies show up at this building."

"And the fifth floor has your back," Jessica added firmly from where she was leaning against the dresser. "We'll keep an eye out everywhere else on campus."

Isabel stood up, brushing off her jeans, her warm expression settling into calm, decisive RA authority. She looked over at Jessica.

"Jessica, text me that photo of him," Isabel said firmly. "Normally, anyone on campus with a Poly Pass can access Bennett Hall—just like the other hillside dorms—from 10:00 AM to 11:59 PM daily so they can tap their card on the reader at the first-floor doors at the bottom of the hill on the quad side, ride our elevator up one level, and walk out the second-floor doors at the top of the hill on the Washington Street side to skip climbing the slope. And from 12:00 AM to 9:59 AM, you have to be a Bennett Hall resident or a signed-in guest who stays escorted at all times by whoever signed you in. As of tonight, Tom just lost both privileges for Bennett Hall. As soon as I step out of here, I'm filing an incident report and forwarding that photo straight to Kelsey Brennan, our Hall Director. Kelsey has the administrative authority to have Campus Access Control strip his Poly Pass of that 10:00 AM to 11:59 PM pass-through access on Bennett Hall's exterior doors, and she'll put out an official building-wide staff bulletin to the front desk and every RA across all six floors barring him from ever being signed in as a guest. If Tom tries to tap his Poly Pass at Bennett Hall on either side of the hill—day or night—his card will flash red, and if he tries to loiter by the doors, Campus Security will trespass him off the quad on the spot. He can hike the hill outside in the rain. This building is your home, and he is not welcome in it."

Lauren let out a long, trembling breath, her shoulders dropping two inches as the weight of fearing an ambush in her own hallway evaporated. "Thank you, Isabel."

"Of course, sweetheart," Isabel smiled gently, giving Lauren's shoulder a warm squeeze. "That's what I'm here for. Never just for the paperwork—for *you* guys."

As the adrenaline of the confrontation began to wear off, the exhaustion of the emotional whiplash finally settled over the room. It was getting late. Ashley, Emily, Cassie, Jessica, and Chloe began gathering their things to head back upstairs to Suite 505/507.

"Before you guys go," Aubrie announced, stepping into the center aisle. She looked at Lauren, then at Sarah, and finally down at Jordan. She shared a brief, unspoken look of absolute, radical trust with him. He nodded immediately, a warm, supportive smile on his face.

"We built something really special in this suite," Aubrie said softly, looking around the room. "We built a family. And families protect each other. So, Jordan and I want to make something perfectly clear. His bed is a safe haven. From now on, it is open to *any* girl in this pact who needs comfort, who feels unsafe, or who just doesn't want to be alone. No questions asked."

It was the ultimate expression of the Constellation they had built—pure, platonic, emotional security, completely devoid of jealousy or condition.

Mark, standing in the doorway, nodded slowly. He didn't look threatened; he looked deeply moved. He fully understood the profound, healing weight of what Aubrie was offering.

Hannah leaned against Mark's side, wrapping her arm around his waist. "That is the most beautiful thing I've ever heard," Hannah smiled warmly. "I'm going to lovingly opt out, of course, because I've got Mark. But it's an incredible offer."

Chloe, grabbing her backpack, smiled sympathetically from the hallway. "Same here. I've got my own guy to worry about, but you guys are seriously the best friends anyone could ask for. Goodnight, everyone."

With a final round of hugs, the five girls headed up the stairwell to the fifth floor.

Sarah, however, stayed sitting on the edge of the bed. She looked completely drained, the confident Australian spark temporarily dimmed by the sheer exhaustion of the breakup.

"You're not going upstairs tonight, Sarah," Lauren said firmly, wiping her eyes. "You're staying here with us."

"I don't want to take anyone's bed," Sarah hesitated, glancing nervously at Isabel as if worried about breaking an overnight guest rule.

Isabel immediately shook her head with a warm, reassuring smile. "Don't even think about the guest log, Sarah. Stay right here with your friends tonight."

"You're taking mine," Lauren insisted. "You can have Bed C in 216. It's the top bunk. It's quiet, and it's safe."

Sarah looked at Lauren, incredibly touched. "Are you sure? Where are you going to sleep?"

Lauren looked over her shoulder at Jordan. He was already maneuvering his wheelchair closer to the head of the bed, patting the mattress next to him with a welcoming smile.

"I'm going to take Aubrie up on her offer," Lauren said softly.

Isabel picked up her clipboard from the dresser and paused at the hallway door, looking back at the quiet, fiercely loyal family settling into Suite 214/216.

"I'm right across the hall in 215," Isabel said softly, her eyes lingering warmly on Lauren, Sarah, and Jordan. "My phone stays on all night. If any of you have a nightmare, or need tea, or just need someone to talk to at three in the morning—knock on my door. Goodnight, guys."

"Goodnight, Isabel. Thank you for everything," Aubrie said warmly as their RA pressed the **PUSH TO OPEN** button to unlock and open the door, stepped out into the hall, and let the motorized opener close and re-lock it behind her.

The sleeping arrangements for the night naturally fell into place, an organic deployment of their fortress. In Room 216, Mark and Hannah claimed Hannah's single Twin XL (Bed A). Zoë climbed into her practical lower bunk (Bed B), and Sarah gratefully climbed up into Lauren's top bunk (Bed C).

In Room 214, the dynamic was just as seamless. Madison climbed up into her top bunk (Bed C), and Aubrie settled into her lower bunk (Bed B), reaching out to click off the overhead light.

Lauren changed into a spare set of pajamas Aubrie lent her and climbed into the accessible Twin XL (Bed A) next to Jordan. He had already scooted flush against the cinderblock wall to give her as much room as possible.

Lauren lay on her side, facing him in the dim amber light of the streetlamp filtering through the blinds. Jordan reached out, gently wrapping his arm around her shoulders in a fiercely protective, brotherly embrace.

Lauren closed her eyes, letting out a long, shaky exhale. Her phone was completely powered down and shoved at the bottom of her backpack. She was surrounded by women who supported her, a compassionate RA right across the hall who had barred her ex from the building, a friend's boyfriend who respected her, and a boy who had offered his own bed to make sure she felt secure. As she listened to the quiet, synchronized breathing of Suite 214, the toxic memory of Tom completely faded away.

She was safe.

## The Sensory Awakening

### Scene 1: The Canadian Wake-Up

**Date:** 2014-08-27 at 07:00 America/New_York

Jordan woke slowly, the dim morning light filtering through the single window of Room 214. The recurring, terrifying nightmare of falling was completely absent. Instead, he felt a deep, soothing warmth pressed against his side. He blinked, his poor vision struggling to focus, until the soft features and long, reddish-brown hair of the girl sleeping next to him resolved into shapes he recognized.

It was Lauren. She was sleeping peacefully in his accessible single Twin XL bed, a physical manifestation of the promise they had made the night before.

As Jordan shifted slightly, Lauren stirred. She opened her warm brown eyes and offered him a soft, sleepy smile. "Good morning, J," she whispered.

She reached down to the floor beside the bed and pulled up a thick, knitted winter hat from her overnight bag. "I was freezing last night before I got under the covers," she said, holding the hat up for him to see. "I was almost tempted to put my favorite toque on."

Jordan stared at the knitted fabric, his brow furrowing in confusion. He slowly reached over to his Vanguard Pro laptop resting on the nightstand, pulling it securely onto his lap. Bypassing the EchoType speech software so as not to wake the others, he launched his sleek, dark-themed Aura Glade terminal. He opened NeoEdit, scaling the bright, neon-green text up massively against the stark black background.

<aac>What is a toque?</aac> he typed, angling the screen so she could read it.

Lauren let out a quiet, melodic laugh. "Oh, right! It's what we call a winter beanie. I'm a dual citizen, Jordan. I grew up in Canada."

Jordan's eyes widened. He had spent his entire life trapped in a small, isolated bubble in Hampton Roads. The realization that he was currently sharing a bed with someone from an entirely different country was dizzying.

Before he could process this international revelation, Lauren leaned forward. With a natural, unhesitating warmth, she pressed a gentle, lingering kiss to his cheek.

At that exact moment, the springs on the lower bunk across the room creaked. Aubrie sat up from Bed B, rubbing the sleep from her eyes.

Jordan froze. The heavy, restrictive programming of his mother's world instantly flared to life in his chest. *Jealousy. Anger. Punishment.* He had just been kissed by another girl in his own bed, and Aubrie had seen the entire thing. He squeezed his eyes shut, his shoulders bracing for the inevitable screaming.

But the room remained completely quiet.

He opened his eyes to see Aubrie walking toward them. Her face wasn't twisted in anger; it was glowing with a serene, profound happiness. She knelt beside his wheelchair and leaned over the bed. Without a single word, she pressed her own warm, loving kiss to his other cheek.

Aubrie caught Lauren's eye, and the two women shared a brilliant, silent smile. The constellation was working perfectly.

### Scene 2: The Australian Voice

**Date:** 2014-08-27 at 07:15 America/New_York

The morning routine kicked into gear. With a gentle, clinical respect that continued to amaze him, Aubrie and Lauren helped Jordan navigate the difficult process of getting out of his pajamas and into his day clothes.

Just as they were finishing, a soft knock echoed from the connecting bathroom door.

"Come in!" Aubrie called out.

The door opened, and Sarah Braithwaite stepped into Room 214. She was fully dressed, her dark brown hair pulled back into her signature messy ponytail.

"Morning, everyone," Sarah smiled, her fierce intelligence bright even at this early hour. She looked directly at Lauren. "I just wanted to say a massive thank you for letting me crash in your bed last night. I love my suitemates upstairs, but being down here with you guys was exactly what I needed after that absolute nightmare with Tom."

"Anytime, Sarah," Lauren beamed. "You're always welcome."

Sarah turned her dark, almost black eyes toward Jordan. Without missing a beat, she walked over to his wheelchair and wrapped her arms around his shoulders in a tight, fierce hug.

Jordan hugged her back, his eyes darting immediately to Aubrie. The girl who had held him through his meltdown was just smiling as she gathered her textbooks. It defied all of his legacy programming. How could she not be mad? How could any of this be real?

He pulled his Vanguard Pro back onto his lap, his fingers trembling slightly as he opened his EchoType software. He needed to express how overwhelming and beautiful this lack of jealousy was.

As his fingers hovered over the keyboard, Madison peeked her head over the edge of the upper bunk, Bed C, her bright blue eyes full of cheerful energy. "Morning, suite family!" she chirped, hopping down the wooden ladder.

She walked straight over to Jordan and planted a happy, affectionate kiss right on his cheek.

Sarah, watching this easy display of affection, felt a surge of belonging. She smiled, stepped forward, and placed a firm, equally affectionate kiss on Jordan's other cheek.

Jordan’s heart hammered against his ribs. He quickly typed his sentence and moved his cursor over the execute button. But he hesitated.

He looked up at Sarah. He was using the "Sydney" voice profile—a crisp, synthesized Australian accent that he had chosen specifically as a quiet act of rebellion against his mother's forced use of a grating, robotic male voice. He was suddenly terrified. What if Sarah felt mocked? What if the Australian girl was deeply offended that he was appropriating her accent through a computer program?

Taking a deep breath, he clicked the execute key.

<aac>I am so incredibly happy to be going to class with all of you today,</aac> the clear, bright Australian-accented voice announced to the room.

Sarah blinked, her head tilting slightly as she processed the synthesized cadence. Jordan tensed, waiting for the fierce temper he knew she possessed to flare up.

Instead, a massive, delighted grin broke across Sarah's face.

"Mate!" Sarah laughed, stepping forward to throw her arms around his neck in another fierce hug. "Is that your voice? That is absolutely brilliant! It sounds just like home!"

Jordan let out a massive, shaky breath of pure relief, a genuine smile finally matching hers.

"Alright, team," Zoë announced, stepping through the bathroom door from Room 216, her analytical gray eyes sweeping the room. "The emotional bonding sequence is complete. If we wish to secure a table at Kincaid Commons before the 8:00 AM rush, we must depart immediately."

"She's right," Madison said, grabbing her backpack. "Let's go get some breakfast!"

### Scene 3: Primary Care

**Date:** 2014-08-27 at 15:30 America/New_York

The CPI Student Health Center was a bustling, brightly lit clinic on the edge of the campus quad. Jordan sat in his wheelchair in the crowded waiting room, his sleek Vanguard Pro resting securely on his lap. He was vibrating with a nervous, hopeful energy. In his limited, highly sheltered understanding of how the world worked, he assumed this was a one-stop shop. He fully expected to walk through the clinic doors and roll back out an hour later wearing a pair of glasses and hearing aids.

He wasn't alone. Aubrie sat in the chair immediately to his left, while Mark and Madison leaned against the wall nearby, acting as a supportive, physical barrier against the chaotic energy of the waiting room.

"Jordan Fuller?" a triage nurse called out from a heavy wooden door leading to the back hallways.

Jordan quickly unlocked his brakes, expecting the familiar, protective phalanx of his family to move with him. He rolled forward a few inches, but when he looked over his shoulder, Mark, Madison, and Aubrie hadn't moved.

Jordan stopped dead. His hands clamped down on his push-rings, his knuckles turning white. A cold, sharp spike of panic hit his chest. He frantically pulled his laptop open, his fingers flying across the keys to pull up his dark-themed NeoEdit terminal.

<aac>You said you wouldn't leave me,</aac> the bright green text read, his screen angled desperately toward Aubrie.

Aubrie immediately stood up and stepped in front of his chair, recognizing the rising tide of a sensory and emotional crash from her own lived experience. She knelt so they were eye-to-eye.

"We aren't leaving you, J," she said, her voice soft but steady, making sure to enunciate clearly so he could read her lips and catch the muffled sounds. "But we have to stay out here. It's a clinic rule for privacy. Only the patient is allowed in the exam rooms."

Jordan stared at her, his breathing turning shallow. He had never done a medical appointment without his mother hovering over him, controlling the narrative and dictating his reality. The thought of rolling through those doors entirely alone was terrifying.

Mark stepped up next to Aubrie, offering a warm, reassuring smile. "You're an adult now, brother. You've got this. We are going to be sitting right in these exact chairs when you come back out. We're not moving an inch."

"We're right here," Madison added cheerfully, giving his shoulder a gentle, grounding squeeze.

Jordan looked at the heavy wooden door, then back at his three friends. They believed in him. He swallowed hard, forcing his hands to loosen their death grip on the wheels. He gave a small, shaky nod, turned his chair around, and rolled through the doors by himself.

He was guided into a small, sterile exam room. A few minutes later, a Nurse Practitioner with a kind face and a tablet stepped in. She glanced at the referral Ms. Higgins had sent over.

"Jordan, hi. I'm Nurse Collins," she said, raising her volume slightly and speaking clearly. "Ms. Higgins sent over some notes regarding your visual and auditory baselines. Let's do a quick screening to get you officially into the university system, and then we'll get you where you need to go."

Jordan typed a quick <aac>Thank you</aac> into his terminal, but his brow furrowed. <aac>Where I need to go?</aac>

The screenings were brief but painfully conclusive. When Nurse Collins asked him to read the eye chart on the wall, Jordan couldn't even make out the large 'E' at the top; to him, it was just a fuzzy, gray smudge against a white background. When she moved to the auditory test, utilizing a basic headset and a series of high-frequency tones, Jordan sat in complete silence. He didn't raise his hand once for any tone above a low, mid-range rumble.

Nurse Collins lowered her tablet, her professional demeanor softening into a look of quiet, profound empathy. She realized instantly that these were not minor deficits; this was years of profound, uncorrected medical neglect.

"Okay, Jordan," she said gently. "You have severe myopia, and what looks like progressive sensorineural hearing loss. Left untreated, it will eventually result in total deafness. I am officially logging these deficits into your CPI medical file, which legally locks in your academic accommodations."

She printed out two slips of paper and handed them to him. "But we are a primary care clinic. We don't dispense hardware here. I am referring you to an excellent dual-practice clinic in Christiansburg—an optometrist and an audiologist right next door to each other in Pepper Plaza. They have the specialized equipment to get you properly fitted."

Jordan looked down at the slips of paper in his lap, the reality of the adult world settling over him. It was a process. But these papers were physical tickets out of the blur and the static.

He wheeled himself back out into the lobby. True to their word, Aubrie, Mark, and Madison were waiting in the exact same spot. Aubrie's face lit up the second she saw him, and she rushed over to give him a warm, grounding hug.

"You did it," she whispered.

Jordan smiled, handing the referrals to Mark.

"Montgomery Vision and Hearing Clinic," Mark read, an easy, confident smile spreading across his face. "I know exactly where this is. It's right next to the Montgomery County Courthouse in Christiansburg. We can catch the Route 1 bus straight down Business 460. We'll take you on Friday."

### Scene 4: The Good Faith Offer

**Date:** 2014-08-27 at 18:30 America/New_York

By the time the group returned from Kincaid Hall after dinner, the chaotic energy of the first three days of classes had settled into a warm, comfortable hum inside Suite 214/216. Jordan sat parked near the center of Room 214 with his Vanguard Pro open on his lap, reviewing his Astronomy 101 syllabus alongside Zoë and Aubrie, while Lauren, Hannah, and Madison were sprawled across the rug and lower bunks comparing notes for the rest of the week.

A polite, rhythmic knock echoed against the heavy wooden door of Room 214.

"Come in!" Hannah called out, setting her highlighter down and reaching over to press the square **PUSH TO OPEN** handicap button on the interior wall.

With a soft click and a mechanical whir, the lock released, the door swung open on its ten-second timer, and Kelsey, the Bennett Hall Residence Director, stepped onto the threshold. The exhausted, frazzled tension that had lined her face on Saturday's move-in day was completely gone, replaced by a relaxed, genuine smile.

"Hi, everyone," Kelsey said, glancing around at the six suitemates. "Do all six of you have a quick minute?"

The girls immediately sat up, and Jordan angled his wheelchair toward the doorway, his hands resting lightly on his keyboard.

"I had a long meeting this afternoon with my boss, the Director of Housing and Residence Life," Kelsey began, stepping just inside the room and folding her hands over her clipboard. "I walked him through the entire situation here in Suite 214 and 216. More importantly, I explained how cooperative, gracious, and mature all six of you have been in handling a database clerical error that was one hundred percent our fault."

She looked directly at Jordan, then at Aubrie and the others.

"First, as a formal apology and a thank-you from the university for not turning our move-in weekend into an administrative disaster, Housing and Dining Services has credited two hundred and fifty dollars in flex dining funds directly to each of your six student accounts."

A collective gasp rippled through the room.

"Are you serious?!" Madison practically bounced off the edge of the lower bunk, her blue eyes wide. "Two hundred and fifty dollars each? Do you have any idea how many Kincaid Hall smoothies and late-night pizzas that is?!"

"We figured a group of freshmen could put it to good use," Kelsey laughed. "But second—and this is the much bigger piece—the Director authorized me to make the six of you a formal institutional offer."

The room quieted instantly, all eyes locked on her.

"As you probably already know from orientation, CPI's rapid enrollment growth means the on-campus housing lottery for sophomore year is brutally competitive," Kelsey explained, her tone turning serious. "Thousands of rising sophomores get lottery-bumped off campus every February. In light of the disruption you six absorbed for us this week, the Office of Housing is offering all six of you a guaranteed on-campus housing contract for next academic year. When housing selection opens in February, your suite will bypass the freshman lottery entirely."

For a second, nobody spoke. The sheer magnitude of the offer hung in the air. For Jordan, whose entire ability to attend classes relied on accessible campus infrastructure and the physical proximity of his chosen family, the terrifying question of what would happen after May had been a quiet shadow at the back of his mind. Now, with a single stroke of a pen, that shadow was gone.

"Let me verify the parameters," Zoë said, her analytical gray eyes sharpening immediately as she sat upright. "You are stating that all six of us are guaranteed on-campus beds for our sophomore year, with zero probabilistic dependency on the February lottery?"

"All six of you are guaranteed a contract," Kelsey confirmed warmly. "Whether you choose to stay together or select another accessible suite configuration on campus, your spots are locked in. It's the best way the university can thank you for making this work."

Jordan's fingers flew across his keyboard, launching his `EchoType` output so Kelsey could hear his answer directly.

<aac>Thank you, Kelsey. We accept,</aac> the crisp, bright Australian voice of his `"Sydney"` profile announced to the room.

"We definitely accept!" Aubrie beamed, reaching over to rest a hand on Jordan's shoulder while Lauren, Hannah, and Madison nodded enthusiastically.

"Wonderful," Kelsey smiled, making a quick notation on her clipboard. "I'll have the formal confirmation letters sent to your CPI student email accounts by tomorrow afternoon, and once the standard two-week room-freeze window closes in September, I'll bring down the final consent paperwork for everyone to sign. Have a great rest of your week, guys."

She pressed the **PUSH TO OPEN** handicap button on the interior wall to unlock and swing the door open, stepping out into the corridor. Ten seconds later, the moment the heavy wooden door swung shut and clicked locked behind her, Madison let out a muffled shriek of triumph into a throw pillow, and Lauren threw her arms around Hannah's neck.

"We beat the lottery before the first week of classes is even over!" Lauren laughed, her Newfoundland cadence ringing out in pure relief.

Jordan looked around the small cinderblock room—at Aubrie smiling down at him, at Zoë already calculating the optimal dining-dollar burn rate, and at the four women celebrating by the bunks. This wasn't just a temporary clerical fluke anymore. They had a future here.

## The Pepper Plaza Expedition

### Scene 1: Route 1 to Pepper Plaza

**Date:** 2014-08-29 at 13:15 America/New_York

By Friday afternoon, the late-August sun was warm and bright over the gray Ironstone buildings of Commonwealth Polytechnic Institute. With their first full week of classes officially behind them, the entire nine-person crew—Aubrie, Madison, Zoë, Lauren, Hannah, Mark, Sarah, and Jessica—formed their familiar protective phalanx around Jordan's manual wheelchair as they rolled down the paved walkway toward the curbside transit bays along **Commonwealth Mall** beneath **The Keystone Bridge** outside **Kincaid Hall**.

Today wasn't just a trip into town for Jordan's vision and hearing appointments. It was a threshold he had never crossed in eighteen years of life: **his very first time riding a bus.**

As they waited by the shelter, Jordan's hands tightened instinctively on his push-rims, his shoulders tensing as a lifetime of painful muscle memory surfaced. Back in Virginia Beach, leaving the house had meant enduring the rough, humiliating ordeal of getting in and out of his mother's luxury sedan. Because Aubrie Sinclair refused to buy a wheelchair-accessible van—claiming it would look "unsightly" in her driveway—every single car trip had begun with a physical struggle. She would park too close to the curb, grab Jordan roughly under the arms before he was braced, and shove and twist his body across the narrow door sill into the low front passenger seat, bruising his hips against the seatbelt buckle while sighing loudly about how much he was *"ruining her back."* Then he would have to sit helplessly inside the hot car listening to her wrench the pins on his wheelchair, slam the folded frame violently into the trunk, and complain for the entire drive about what an exhausting ordeal it was to take him anywhere.

Whenever he had looked out the car window at a city bus passing by, his mother had weaponized that, too—telling him that public buses were *"dirty, dangerous, and ten times rougher,"* and that if he ever tried to board one in a wheelchair, he would get stuck on the ramp while a furious bus driver and fifty impatient commuters glared at him for holding up traffic.

Then a deep, refined diesel hum echoed beneath the stone arch of the skybridge.

Gliding smoothly up to the curb was a sixty-foot **Blue Ridge Transit** low-floor articulated bus manufactured by **Prairie Coachworks**, painted in crisp Arctic White with an Appalachian Pine Green lower skirt and sweeping Teal and Sky-Blue side ribbons. Above the wide panoramic windshield, the glowing amber LED destination sign alternated cleanly between two frames:

`1 CBURG TRANS CTR`
`via S MAIN ST`

The transit operator—a friendly middle-aged man in a short-sleeved Blue Ridge Transit polo—spotted Jordan's wheelchair on the sidewalk immediately. He didn't scowl, check his watch, or sigh. Instead, he offered Jordan a warm, unhurried two-finger wave through the windshield and flipped a toggle switch on his dashboard.

With a gentle, rhythmic pneumatic *hiss*, the entire front end of the sixty-foot bus kneeled downward, dropping the front step until it was nearly flush with the concrete curb. The dual-leaf front boarding doors folded wide open, a soft chime sounded, and the wide, yellow-edged aluminum low-floor ADA ramp flipped smoothly out of the floor and settled onto the sidewalk right beside the driver's compartment. The entire deployment had taken less than four seconds.

"Afternoon, folks! Take your time coming aboard," the driver called out cheerfully.

"Right up the front ramp, brother," Mark said easily, flashing his maroon-and-orange **CPI Poly Pass** to the driver as Jessica and Aubrie flanked Jordan's chair. "These low-floor Prairie Coachworks buses are engineered like tanks. You don't even have to leave your seat."

Jordan took a deep breath, released his wheel locks, and pushed forward onto the textured aluminum ramp.

To his utter astonishment, the incline was barely steeper than a hallway threshold. With a light, effortless assist from Mark and Jessica behind him just to keep his momentum steady, his front casters glided over the lip of the ramp and rolled straight onto the flat, slip-resistant floor of the air-conditioned cabin.

Nobody grabbed him under the arms. Nobody wrenched his torso across a car seat or bruised his ribs. Nobody took his wheelchair away from him and slammed it into a dark trunk like unwanted luggage. He remained seated upright in his own chair, in full control of his own body.

Just behind the front wheel well on the passenger side, the driver flipped up a padded three-person forward bench with a single fluid motion, exposing a spacious, dedicated wheelchair securement bay. Mark guided Jordan in backing his chair flush against the padded backrest, knelt down, and pulled the four self-tensioning floor retractor straps out of their tracks—*click, click, click, click*—hooking them cleanly onto the frame of Jordan's chair and snapping the lap belt comfortably across his waist.

"Locked and loaded in thirty-five seconds flat," Mark grinned, patting Jordan's shoulder.

One by one, the rest of the group flashed their Poly Passes to the driver and filled the surrounding seats—Aubrie and Madison sitting right beside Jordan's armrests, Lauren and Sarah across the wide aisle, and Zoë, Hannah, Mark, and six-foot-three Jessica claiming the row immediately behind him.

With another smooth pneumatic whisper, the ramp folded back into the floor, the front suspension rose to ride height, and the sixty-foot articulated coach glided effortlessly away from the Commonwealth Mall curb. As they turned onto South Main Street and cruised south down the four-lane stretch of **U.S. Route 460 Business** toward Christiansburg, Jordan marveled at how velvet-smooth the air-ride suspension felt beneath his tires. Every bump in the asphalt was absorbed into a gentle, cradle-like sway.

Jordan opened his `Pinnacle Vanguard Pro` on his lap tray. Launching `NeoEdit` on his Aura Glade terminal, he typed out a message in large neon-green text and angled the screen toward Aubrie, Madison, and Mark:

<aac>That took less than a minute. Nobody had to lift me, and nobody had to fold my chair into a trunk. My mother told me getting on a bus was impossible and that I would hold up traffic for twenty minutes.</aac>

Aubrie reached over and laced her fingers warmly through his, her eyes shining with quiet emotion as she saw the weight lifting off his shoulders. "Everything she told you to keep you trapped in that house was a lie, J. Out here, the world actually has room for you."

"Your mum talked a lot of rubbish, mate," Sarah agreed from across the aisle, shaking her head in her thick Sydney accent. "Whether you're in Sydney or right here in Blacksburg, low-floor buses are built so you can go wherever you want, whenever you want, with zero dramas."

"And look out the left window when we crest this hill," Mark pointed as the bus glided past the Christiansburg town limits and turned toward the commercial district. Even through Jordan's blurry, uncorrected vision, he could make out the brick-and-limestone silhouette of a large civic building across from a bustling shopping center. "That's the **Montgomery County Courthouse** on the left, and right here on the right is **Pepper Plaza**."

Hannah reached up and pressed the yellow ADA stop-request strip beside Jordan's bay, triggering a polite chime overhead.

As the bus pulled into the **Pepper Plaza Transit Center** bay, Jordan braced slightly out of old habit—half-expecting getting *off* the bus to be harder than getting on.
